The runtime instance the grant opens is **projection-only**: it runs exactly one command, refuses an ordinary `mutate` for its whole lifetime (returns `denied`, exactly as for a caller with no grant), and is discarded — never returned to a caller — after that command. Proven on the real stack: the founding grant projects five records, the membership confirms, it queries back, and an ordinary `mutate` through the same handle after the projection is denied. Recorded in `VPS-F001`'s Workspace-creation section, `VPS-A002`'s edge conventions, and G05.

---

### F197 — FDN-85's reconciliation log is bespoke, pending FDN-68's audit interface

Recorded as a limitation during FDN-85's Stage 0 ruling, not a defect. Counted like F76, F157 and F185.

FDN-85's done criteria require reconciliation to be "auditable." A typed `AuditEntry` graph node is not available — FDN-53 deferred all audit-node writing to `VPS-F004` (F135), and FDN-68 owns the typed audit interface, which does not exist yet. Building a one-off audit writer inside FDN-85 would repeat the mistake F132 (edge storage), F133 (Gate 2) and F135 (audit writing) were each kept out of their stage to avoid: inventing infrastructure under stage pressure that deserves its own design pass.

So "auditable" here means a **deterministic, replayable structured log** of every reconciliation run and every projection write, each carrying the `privileged-projection-exception` marker that distinguishes it from an ordinary authorized write. **When FDN-68 lands, this log is retrofitted to emit through FDN-68's typed audit interface** rather than staying a permanent bespoke format. Recorded here, and in FDN-85's done-criteria writeback, so the retrofit is not forgotten as "temporary."

---


### F199 — the local-first, device-canonical architecture is reversed

**Founder decision, 20 September 2026.** After the six-week foundation build, a review weighed what the architecture would cost every feature built on it. Findings that decided it:

- **Feature cost.** 46 of 78 feature specifications depend on Tier 1 or Tier 3 behavior or on work running "on an authorized device". Stateful workflows needed bespoke conflict designs under offline CRDT merge. Estimated effect: roughly double the effort per feature, more for the financial features.
- **Server capability.** Margin alerts, scheduled payroll reports, exports, integrations, the Sync API and the intelligence engine could run only while an authorized person's device was online, because the server could not read rate cards or pay data.
- **Business continuity.** Tier 1 key loss without two of three recovery holders would permanently lose payroll history, and support could not help. In a small agency the Owner is usually the only finance holder.
- **Offline benefit already given up.** Cold restart and tab reload required an online unlock; only one tab synchronized.
- **No commercial pull.** `VPS-003` mentioned privacy, encryption or local-first zero times. The E2E promise was also weaker than stated, since a web server delivers the code that holds the keys.

**What replaces it** — `VPS-A003` rewritten: PostgreSQL is canonical; `services/api` is the only writer; named mutations with idempotency and stale-state rejection; Electric replicates each person's permitted Tier 0 slice into device SQLite through sync shapes generated from `VPS-A004`'s policy table; Tier 1 and Tier 2 are field-encrypted under a KMS key hierarchy, fetched on demand, held in memory only, and audited on every read; cryptographic erasure is kept for Tier 1 through per-erasure-domain data keys; Tier 3 is kept as a deferred end-to-end module. Offline: the product now boots offline from cache, reads all Tier 0 data and queues Tier 0 writes.

**What is preserved.** The node and edge registry, privacy classes, tier assignments, the permission matrix and its rules, subject exclusion, k-anonymity, the design system, the CI pipeline, authentication and device trust. The retired implementation — `services/sync-engine`, the Loro Worker runtime and materialization, the sealed store, Tier 1 envelopes, identity transfer and threshold recovery, and the Tier 3 root code — is preserved on branch `archive/local-first-e2e` at `60c2745`. FDN-68's uncommitted audit work at the time of the decision is not on that branch and remains in the founder's working tree.

**Findings this supersedes.** F120 (a Loro document too new to open), F140 (Worker availability states), F141 (local author versus arriving peer) and F186 (per-recipient relay filtering) describe mechanisms that no longer exist; each is closed as superseded, and F186's underlying requirement is now met structurally — Tier 1 and Tier 2 never replicate, and Tier 0 replication is filtered per person by generated streams. F125 (a backdated `managed_by` move) remains open, moved from the Movable Tree to `org.moveEmployee`. F118 (the SyncStatus render) remains open, now with the added `NeedsAttention` state. F127's open question — which channel delivers a live role change — is answered: the sync connection.

**Documents changed:** `CLAUDE.md`, `VPS-002`, `VPS-003`, `VPS-A001`, `VPS-A002`, `VPS-A003` (rewritten), `VPS-A004`, `VPS-A005`, `VPS-A006`, `VPS-A007`, `VPS-F001`, `VPS-F007`. The remaining feature specifications that assume client-side protected processing, the retention window or the Movable Tree are corrected feature by feature under a tracked Linear sweep, each before that feature is built.

### F200 — trust becomes a program of verifiable promises

**Founder decision, 20 September 2026, with F199.** `VPS-A008` is new. It replaces "Vulto cannot read your data" with eight promises a customer can check: no staff access without Owner approval; a customer-visible Access Transparency log; field-level encryption of pay and HR data; customer-managed keys on Enterprise; a hash-chained, daily-anchored audit log with a published verifier; the permission policy published and its running hash shown in the product; an open export format; and a residency region per workspace on Enterprise. It fixes external-assurance triggers (penetration test and disclosure policy before the first real-data pilot; SOC 2 Type I readiness at the first paying customer). `VPS-003`'s Enterprise plan gains customer-managed keys and residency choice.


### F201 — the replacement sync engine is Electric, not PowerSync

Raised and closed on 20 September 2026, hours after F199, when the founder asked the direct question: *is PowerSync paid, and if so replace it.*

**What checking found.** PowerSync's client SDKs are Apache-2.0 and its Open Edition service is free to run, but the service is licensed under the **Functional Source License**, which permits any use except a "Competing Use" — defined partly as a product offering "substantially similar functionality". Vulto is an HR product, not a sync product, so the permission holds on a fair reading; the objection is that it is a reading, and a pre-revenue company should not carry a license question it may one day have to argue. More concretely, the Open Edition requires **MongoDB** for its sync buckets in addition to PostgreSQL: a second datastore to run, back up, restore and keep alive in every residency region `VPS-A008` P8 promises.

**Decision.** Electric (Apache-2.0) for the read path. It replicates PostgreSQL to clients over HTTP as filtered shapes, needs nothing but the database it replicates from, and is self-hostable anywhere. Electric has no write path and no local store, which is acceptable because writes already go through Vulto's named mutations and the local cache was always ours to shape: `packages/graph` keeps the SQLite-WASM cache, and adds the outbox with idempotent retry, optimistic application and rollback, and SyncStatus. Roughly two weeks of TypeScript in exchange for one fewer datastore, no license ambiguity, and a stack a customer's own infrastructure team could run.

**What this changes in the documents.** `VPS-A001`'s sync client selection and A001-T02/T04; `VPS-A003`'s topology and its replication decision; `VPS-A007`'s development image, compose stack, deploy order and A007-T19; `CLAUDE.md`'s stack and phase list. `wa-sqlite` stays in the stack; `loro-crdt` is still retired.

**What does not change.** Nothing about tiers, permissions, encryption, offline behavior or the trust program. The shape conformance gate, the publication allowlist and "no Tier 1 or Tier 2 data on a device" all stand; only the component streaming the rows is different.


### F202 — the sync audience, and three facts closed before implementation

Closed 21 September 2026, before implementation began, so the implementing agent has nothing to decide.

**The sync audience.** Electric serves single-table shapes with a where clause, and supports subqueries whose dependencies it tracks. Generating a different shape per role and scope would have meant re-expressing `own`, direct-report and subject-exclusion scope as SQL where clauses — a second implementation of the permission rules. Instead the interceptor writes an explicit audience of identifiers (`sync_node_audience`, `sync_edge_audience`), maintained in the transaction of every mutation and recomputed per workspace on any membership, role or `managed_by` change; every device subscribes to the same two shapes filtered by it, through an authenticated API proxy that sets every shape parameter server-side. The publication allowlist gains the two audience tables, which carry identifiers only. `VPS-A003` A003-T57, A003-T58 and new A003-T72; `VPS-A007` gate 6 and A007-T03.

**Local key provider.** Development and CI cannot call AWS KMS. A `LocalKeyProvider` behind the same `KeyProvider` interface takes its root key from the environment; production refuses to start with it. A003-T73.

**Browser storage and tabs.** OPFS synchronous access is unavailable in a SharedWorker, and one cache shared by every tab is required (A003-T66). The web cache therefore uses `wa-sqlite`'s `IDBBatchAtomicVFS` inside a SharedWorker, falling back to a dedicated Worker holding a `navigator.locks` lease where SharedWorker is unavailable. `VPS-A001`.

### F203 — the CI Postgres service cannot be started with logical replication as specified

Raised 21 September 2026 while implementing Stage 1 of the server-authoritative build.

**What the brief requires.** Postgres must run with `wal_level=logical`, `max_replication_slots=10` and `max_wal_senders=10` locally and "wherever Postgres is started" in CI, because Electric replicates from the write-ahead log (F201). Locally this is the `command` on the compose service, and it works: `SHOW wal_level` returns `logical`.

**What CI allows.** `.github/workflows/slow-lane.yml` starts Postgres in two jobs as a `services:` container. A service container is configured with `image`, `env`, `ports`, `volumes` and `options`. The `options` string is passed to `docker create` ahead of the image name, so there is no place to give the container a command or arguments. The three settings therefore cannot be supplied the way the compose file supplies them.

**What was not done in Stage 1.** No workaround was chosen: each candidate changes how CI starts its database, which is a pipeline decision. The slow lane was left as it was.

**Consequence.** None yet. No CI suite touches logical replication until Stage 6 adds the Electric-backed browser suite, and Stage 7 wires it into the slow lane, so the decision is needed before Stage 6 finishes, not before Stage 2 starts.

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 21 September 2026.** CI gets a small Postgres image of its own, `FROM` the digest the workflows already use, whose default command carries the three flags. It is built and pinned exactly as the CI image is, and every Postgres service container then uses it. Implemented in Stage 6 item 0 of `docs/Claude_Code_Build_Prompt.md`, because no CI suite needs replication before then.

### F204 — a `User` node has no workspace, but `graph_nodes` requires one

Raised 21 September 2026, at the start of Stage 2 of the server-authoritative build.

**The conflict.** `VPS-A002`'s Universal node conventions give every node a `workspace_id` except two: the Workspace itself, and the `User`, which "carries none — a person may hold WorkspaceMembership in more than one workspace; scoping and role live on the membership, not the identity". The Stage 2 brief makes `graph_nodes.workspace_id` `not null` and `node_id` the primary key, and says the Workspace node's `workspace_id` equals its own `node_id`. The Workspace case is solved by that rule. The `User` case is not: a person who creates or joins a second workspace has one `User` node and no single workspace to put in the column.

**Why this was not decided in code.** Every candidate changes the schema or the sync design: a null `workspace_id` for `User` rows, a separate row per workspace under a different `node_id`, or keeping `User` out of `graph_nodes` (which breaks the `membership_of` edge's foreign key to it). The columns, indexes and the shape filter in Stage 6 all follow from the choice.

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 21 September 2026.** The recommendation above — a null `workspace_id` for `User` rows — was rejected, because a row that belongs to no workspace breaks per-workspace residency (`VPS-A008` P8), export and erasure, and would let one workspace's edits to a person's node appear in another's. Instead a `User` is stored once per workspace it belongs to, under the same `node_id` (its Better Auth account id). `graph_nodes` has the composite primary key `(workspace_id, node_id)`, every other node type's `node_id` stays globally unique through a partial index, and edges reference their endpoints by `(workspace_id, node_id)`, so an edge can never join two workspaces. `VPS-A002` and `VPS-A003` were amended on `main`. Built in Stage 2.

### F205 — the anonymous contribution nodes have no `created_at`, but `graph_nodes` requires one

Raised 21 September 2026, building Stage 2's round-trip test for every registered node type.

**The conflict.** The Stage 2 brief defines `graph_nodes.created_at timestamptz not null`. `VPS-A002`'s universal conventions list a closed set of omissions, and `PulseAggregateContribution` and `WellnessAggregateContribution` omit `created_at`, `created_by`, `updated_at`, `updated_by`, `soft_deleted_at` and `soft_deleted_by`, keeping only `is_soft_deleted`. The omission is deliberate: an exact creation time would let a contribution be matched to the person who submitted it. `records.ts` enforces it, and `VPS-A007` gate 3 tests it. Both types are Tier 0, so they belong in `graph_nodes`.

**Why this was not decided in code.** Putting any value in the column, such as the epoch or the insert time, is either a fabricated fact or the very timestamp the omission exists to withhold. Making the column nullable is the obvious fix, but it changes the schema the brief specified and is a privacy-relevant choice.

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 21 September 2026.** The recommendation was accepted: `graph_nodes.created_at` is nullable, and a check constraint allows null only for `PulseAggregateContribution` and `WellnessAggregateContribution`, so no other type can omit it. This follows `VPS-A002`'s closed omission policy and keeps those contributions uncorrelatable. Built in migration `0011`; the store no longer refuses the two types, and the round-trip test covers every registered node type.

### F206 — support and system principals have no audit shape and no policy rows

Raised 21 September 2026, building Stage 3 of the server-authoritative build.

**What is specified.** `VPS-A004` A004-T22 requires support and system principals to be evaluated by the interceptor against their own policy rows, and every decision they receive to be audited. `VPS-A004` and `VPS-A003` name four system principals (`audience-recompute`, `retention-sweep`, `erasure`, `key-rotation`).

**What is not.**
- **The audit shape.** The `AuditEntry` contract in `VPS-F004` records an actor as `actor_user_id` and `actor_membership_id`, both UUIDs of a person. A support grant has neither, and a system job is not a user. There is no field that can name them.
- **The support scope.** `VPS-A008` A008-T02 says an access request states "scope" and `VPS-A008` describes it as which workspace areas, read or write. "Areas" is not defined against the registry, so a support principal's policy row cannot be written.
- **System operations.** No document lists what each system principal may do, so "permitted operations are enumerated in the policy table" has nothing to enumerate.

**What was built without deciding.** The `Principal` union has all three kinds. The policy table holds the system principals' names and one operation, `audit.pseudonymize-actor` for `erasure`, because that operation has code and `VPS-F004` G06 states it. A support or system principal resolves to no node grant, and asking the interceptor to audit one throws `NonMemberAuditUnsupportedError`, so nothing is ever released without its entry.

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 21 September 2026.** The recommendation was accepted, with three additions.
1. `AuditEntry` gains `actor_kind` (`member`, `support`, `system`). `actor_user_id` and `actor_membership_id` stay required for a member; a support entry carries `actor_grant_id`; a system entry carries `actor_system_name` from the closed enum. `audit_journal` gained the same columns and a check that exactly the matching identifier is present. **`VPS-F004`'s AuditEntry contract was corrected accordingly** (new G08).
2. A support scope is `{ access: "read" | "read-write", node_types }`, evaluated against the same policy table as a member and capped at the scope, never above Owner. A support principal may never write `Workspace`, `WorkspaceMembership`, `User` or `AuditEntry`, never change roles, and never reach a Tier 3 type, whatever its scope lists. `VPS-A008`'s "areas" means node types.
3. Each stage adds the system operations it builds: Stage 5 adds `key-rotation` and `erasure`'s key destruction; Stage 6 adds `audience-recompute`.

### F207 — `Client`'s write authority disagrees between `VPS-A002` and `VPS-F008`

Raised 21 September 2026, building Stage 3's write-authority gate.

**The disagreement.** `VPS-A002`'s Cross-Suite Node Ownership table (and its `OWNERSHIP_REGISTRY` in `packages/schema`) makes Vulto Sales the permanent owner of `Client` and Projects the bootstrap writer "until Sales activates; Sales thereafter". `VPS-F008`'s write-authority policy lists `Client` as "Not applicable. Roster was never authorized to write it, so there is no authority to hand off", and its `ApplicationActivation` enumerates only the four applications that own a node type Roster bootstraps, deliberately excluding Sales.

**The reading applied.** `VPS-F008` governs: `Client` is not applicable, so the gate applies no restriction to it. The gate treats an owner outside the four activatable applications as having nothing to hand off to. This is recorded as a finding rather than left as a micro-decision, because it is a real disagreement between two documents. `VPS-A002`'s ownership row and the registry entry need reconciling in the FDN-104 specification sweep.

### F208 — `managed_by` and `scoped_to_entity` have no governing partition, so no reporting-line write can be authorized

Raised 21 September 2026, building Stage 4's `org.moveEmployee`.

**The mechanism.** `VPS-A004` assigns no class to an edge type, so an edge write requires Full on both endpoint node types. Where an endpoint has more than one privacy partition, the edge registry's `governingPartitions` declares which partition governs the edge (F136, founder ruling), and an endpoint with no declaration resolves to `none`, "the conservative default for anything unreviewed".

**The gap.** `managed_by` (Employee to Employee) and `scoped_to_entity` (Employee to Entity) both have an `Employee` endpoint, which has two partitions (`operational`, Tier 0, and `compensation`, Tier 1). Neither edge declares a governing partition. Every role therefore resolves to `none` for every write to them, including the Owner. `assignment_of`, `has_skill` and `holds_certification`, which also connect `Employee`, do declare `operational`.

**Consequence.** `org.moveEmployee` opens and closes `managed_by` edges and can never pass authorization. `VPS-A003` A003-T69 and the Stage 4 done criteria (a cycle-creating move rejected, history half-open and non-overlapping) are unreachable through the pipeline.

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 21 September 2026.** `governingPartitions: { Employee: "operational" }` was declared on both `managed_by` and `scoped_to_entity`, matching `assignment_of` and `has_skill`, and recorded in `VPS-A002`'s `governingPartitions` table with the rationale that a reporting line and an employing entity are operational HR facts, not compensation, so whoever may write an Employee's operational half may move them. The six tests that were skipped pass. `F125` stays open with the current behavior: a backdated move is refused as `invalid-args`, and `VRS-F037` decides backdating when the org chart is built.

### F209 — a cryptographic erasure has no audit event

Raised 21 September 2026, building Stage 5's `erasure.ts`.

**What is specified.** `VPS-A003` "Cryptographic erasure": "`VPS-F004` records that erasure occurred." `VPS-F007` G04 exempts the journal from erasure. The Stage 5 brief has the erasure function call `appendAudit`.

**What is not.** The `AuditEntry` contract in `VPS-F004` (as extended by F206) is a closed vocabulary. Its event types describe access decisions and a privileged projection; its operations are `NodeRead`, `NodeCreate`, `EdgeCreate` and the like; its targets are a node, an edge or a query. Destroying an erasure domain's key is none of these: it is a system operation on a key, concerning a domain that may be a person.

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 21 September 2026.** The recommendation was accepted with one addition. `AuditEntry` gained event type `CryptographicErasureExecuted`, operation `KeyDestroy` and the closed target `ErasureTarget { erasure_domain_id, tier, destroyed_key_count, erasure_request_id }`, written only by the `erasure` system principal, in the same transaction as the key destruction. `erasure_request_id` is null until `VPS-F007`'s ErasureRequest exists, and required from then on, so every erasure traces back to an approved request. `VPS-F004` was corrected (G09). Built in Stage 5: the `ErasureAudit` seam was replaced with the real `appendAudit` call, and a test proves a failed audit destroys no key.

### F210 — dropping `device_unlock_secret` would un-revoke devices

Raised 21 September 2026, building Stage 6's shape proxy, which reads the workspace-scoped revoke.

**The fact.** F191 split device revocation in two so that one tenant cannot destroy another's local data: a workspace Owner's Revoke is scoped to that workspace and is stored as `device_unlock_secret.revoked_at` for `(workspace, user, device)`; global retirement (`device.is_revoked`) belongs to the device's own user. The Stage 7 brief drops `device_unlock_secret` with the retired unlock code. Nothing else stores the workspace-scoped revoke.

**Founder ruling, 21 September 2026 — an amendment to Stage 7's item that drops `device_unlock_secret`.** In Stage 7, before dropping the table:
1. create a control-plane table `device_workspace_revocation (workspace_id, device_id, revoked_at, revoked_by, reason)` with primary key `(workspace_id, device_id)`, identifiers only, never synced;
2. backfill it from `device_unlock_secret` in the same migration;
3. repoint every revoke check, the session path and the shape proxy, to the new table;
4. then drop `device_unlock_secret`;
5. add a test proving a device revoked before the migration is still revoked after it.

Until then the shape proxy reads `device_unlock_secret` (`services/api/src/sync/shape-proxy.ts`), with a comment naming this finding.

### F192 — one of the four diagnostics clients is missing from the production-build exclusion list

Raised at the founder's request during FDN-63 Stage 5, reconciling FDN-52's checkpoint claim that "production artifact search found zero diagnostics matches" against the 8 September audit's claim that a graph-sync diagnostics route is in the production bundle. **Reported only — not fixed here, and not fixed in the FDN-63 branch.**

`apps/roster-web/next.config.ts` excludes diagnostics clients from an optimized build by aliasing them to `false`:

```
"./device-store-diagnostics-client$": false,
"./graph-persistence-diagnostics-client$": false,
"./worker-diagnostics-client$": false,
```

There are **four** diagnostics client components. `./graph-sync-diagnostics-client$` is not in the list.

**Measured, not read off the config.** A clean `next build` reports:

| Route | First-load size |
|---|---|
| `/device-store-diagnostics` | 145 B |
| `/graph-persistence-diagnostics` | 145 B |
| `/worker-diagnostics` | 145 B |
| **`/graph-sync-diagnostics`** | **50.5 kB** |

and `__vultoGraphSyncDiagnostics` is present in the emitted static chunks, where `__vultoGraphPersistenceDiagnostics` and `__vultoDeviceStoreDiagnostics` are absent.

**The founder's specific question, answered: `graph-persistence-diagnostics` IS excluded**, exactly like device-store and worker. The outlier is `graph-sync-diagnostics`, which is precisely what the audit named.

**Scoped accurately rather than dramatically, in both directions.** All four routes carry the same Server Component env gate (`VULTO_DEVICE_STORE_DIAGNOSTICS !== "1"` → `notFound()`), and all four prerender to a 404 in a production build; the graph-sync 404 HTML does not reference its client chunk. So the diagnostics surface is **not reachable through its route** in production — this is a shipped-artifact and defense-in-depth gap, not a live hole. Two things make it worth a finding anyway: the emitted chunk is a static asset that ships with the deployment and is fetchable by URL, and FDN-52's "zero diagnostics matches in the production artifact" is, as a statement about the artifact, false. Separately confirmed as *not* a problem: `createUncheckedLocalGraphClient` — the widened test client that can send the un-permission-checked `apply-delta-batch` — is absent from every production chunk, so F131/F145's containment argument still holds. The `apply-delta-batch` and `erase-local-store` protocol strings do appear in the production Worker bundle, which is expected and harmless: the Worker is one artifact, and no production client can send either message.

**Belongs in its own issue, not FDN-63.** The fix is one alias line, but the finding underneath it is that a four-item exclusion list drifted to three without anything noticing — so the issue should also make the list impossible to forget an entry in (derive it, or fail the build when a `*-diagnostics-client` file has no alias), and correct FDN-52's record.

**Closed by FDN-91.** `./graph-sync-diagnostics-client$: false` was added to the `next.config.ts` alias block, and a clean `next build` now emits no `__vultoGraphSyncDiagnostics` in any chunk — all four diagnostics routes leave 404 shells. The list is no longer trusted: `scripts/artifact-check.mjs` enumerates the `*-diagnostics` routes that exist, requires each one's `./*-client` import to be in the alias block, and fails the build if any diagnostics `window` global or `createUncheckedLocalGraphClient` reaches a production chunk — so a newly added diagnostics route that is not excluded, or a re-introduced diagnostics import, fails the check rather than shipping. The check runs after the production build in FDN-55's slow lane (it needs a real build, so it is not part of hermetic `pnpm verify`). FDN-52's overstated "zero diagnostics matches" claim was corrected on its Linear thread (8 September) and is recorded here. The hermetic-build precondition — `layout.tsx` fetching Inter from `next/font/google` at build time — was fixed in the same issue: `next/font/local` against a committed `inter-latin-wght-normal.woff2`, and a network-isolated `next build` now passes.

---

### F191 — a workspace Owner's Revoke could destroy another tenant's local data on the same device

Surfaced building FDN-63 Stage 3, wiring the new `device` table into the merged F151 revocation signal, and **raised at the Stage 4 checkpoint rather than shipped**.

The merged F151 slice classified `device-revoked` from `device_unlock_secret.revokedAt` — a per-`(workspace, device)` row — so an explicit single-device revocation was workspace-scoped, and its browser test proves "an explicit single-device revocation erases only that device's workspace." FDN-63's canonical `device` table has one row per `(user, application)` and one `is_revoked` boolean: no per-workspace dimension.

The first implementation resolved that by having the Owner's Revoke set `is_revoked` globally, reasoning that `VPS-F001`'s "The Device node is marked revoked" names one node and one flag, and that every realistic reason to revoke — lost, stolen, an ex-employee's laptop — means the device should hold no workspace's data.

**Founder ruling: rejected as built.** The reasoning that overturns it:

* **It is a cross-tenant authority violation, not a scoping preference.** The `device` row spans workspaces. A global flag set by workspace A's Owner destroys workspace B's local data on that device — while A's Owner has no authority over B, no visibility into it, and no way to know B exists. Vulto's market is firms serving multiple clients, so this is a production certainty, not a hypothetical.
* **It silently regressed merged behavior.** The F151 browser test asserting "erases only that device's workspace" was still passing only because it had a single workspace; the property it names was gone. FDN-63's own scope text — "Explicit device revocation: Owner action, **this device only**" — said the same thing.
* **The Modal confirmation is not the safeguard here.** A confirmation protects against a mistaken click by someone with authority. It does nothing about an action taken correctly and deliberately by someone who never had authority over the data it destroys.

**The corrected model — two separately authorized actions, differing in scope, not one action with a broad blast radius:**

1. **Workspace Owner Revoke — workspace-scoped.** `revokeDevice` writes `device_unlock_secret.revokedAt` for the acting Owner's workspace only. It does not touch `device.is_revoked` or the device-global `push_token`. This restores the merged F151 semantics exactly, and those tests pass unmodified.
2. **Global retirement — the device's own user only.** `retireOwnDevice` (`POST /devices/retire`) is the sole path that may set `device.is_revoked`. It is authorized by the account session with no workspace context, because the action has no workspace scope; it also revokes every unlock secret the device holds so the effect is immediate. The device must belong to the session's own user — an Owner cannot reach a colleague's device here, and the denial is non-enumerating. Surfaced in the Devices UI as a distinct action from Owner Revoke, with its own Modal copy naming the wider consequence.
3. **The membership-revocation and account-suspension cascades are audit-only.** Both already revoke unlock secrets at the scope they legitimately own — one workspace, and every workspace of a suspended account respectively — so neither needs `is_revoked`, and leaving it alone keeps the flag meaning exactly one thing: *the device's owner retired it*.

Proven by paired cross-workspace tests at both layers. `auth.integration.test.ts`: an Owner's revoke in workspace A leaves the same device's workspace B unlockable and the global row untouched; the device's own user retiring it blocks unlock in both; an Owner attempting the global path against a colleague's device is denied and changes nothing; the membership cascade leaves the other workspace unlockable. `device-revocation-signal.spec.ts`: one browser context (therefore genuinely one device, one IndexedDB, one generated id) holding two seeded workspaces — after A's Owner revokes, B still opens **with its content intact**; after the user's own retirement, B locks, classifies `device-revoked`, and cannot reopen. Mutation-tested by restoring the global write inside `revokeDevice`, which fails the cross-workspace tests in both suites.

**How this got here, recorded rather than tidied away.** The global model was flagged at the Stage 4 checkpoint as a decision worth pushing back on, with the multi-workspace consultant case named — but it was flagged *as implemented and passing*, with the cross-tenant framing understated as an edge case rather than an authority boundary. The honest lesson is that "this is cheap to change now" is not a substitute for not building it: a security property that depends on a reviewer catching it is one that would have shipped if the review had been lighter.

---

## What did not change

**No permission grant, anywhere.** Every access decision after this pass is one that `VPS-A004`'s matrix already stated. The corrections moved facts to the layer that enforces them and gave duplicate concepts one name each. A reader who knew the intended behavior before would find nothing new in the behavior — only in whether a machine can now confirm it.

**No tier assignment, except in what it is called.** HeadcountSnapshot was Tier 0 and remains Tier 0; it now says that this is deliberate.

### F211 — Electric signals audience removals as `move-out` events, and the client protocol has more parameters than the proxy list named

**Found while:** Stage 6, running the sync browser suite against real Electric 1.8.1 and `@electric-sql/client` 1.5.28.

**Move-out.** `VPS-A003` "Reads" says a person's cache is kept equal to their audience, and the brief's replication item says to apply inserts, updates and deletes. For a shape filtered by a subquery (`node_id IN (SELECT … FROM sync_node_audience …)`), removing a row from the audience does not delete the row in Postgres, so Electric cannot send a `delete`. It sends `{"headers":{"event":"move-out","patterns":[{"pos":1,"value":"<hash>"}]}}`, and each row it delivered carried `headers.tags: ["<pos>/<hash>"]`. The client must remember each row's tags, remove the tags a move-out names, and delete any row left with none. A row moving back in arrives as an ordinary insert.

**Implemented:** `cache_tags (template, row_id, tag)` in the device cache (identifiers only), tag bookkeeping on every change, and move-out handling in `SqliteCache.applyBatch`, in the same transaction as the cursor. Tested by `engine.test.ts::a move-out removes rows whose only tag left, and keeps rows another tag still admits` and by the browser test `removing the member's role removes the rows they may no longer read from the cache`, which fails without it. The tag format `<pos>/<hash>` is observed behavior of the pinned version, not a documented contract: the client library types call a tag an opaque string. Upgrading Electric or the client re-runs both tests, which is the guard.

**Proxy parameters.** The pinned client also sends `expired_handle` and `cache-buster` (cache-busting after a stale handle) and `log=full` (its default). The proxy forwards those and nothing else beyond `offset`, `handle`, `live` and `cursor`; `log` is accepted only as `full`, because `changes_only` would narrow what the shape returns. Everything else, including `table`, `where`, `columns`, `params`, `replica`, `subset__*` and `secret`, is still a `400`.

**Specification correction (applied, 21 September 2026, delegated at the Stage 6 review):** `VPS-A003` T72 lists the forwarded parameters as `offset`, `handle`, `live`, `cursor`; it should add `expired_handle`, `cache-buster` and `log=full`. `VPS-A003` "Reads" should say that a subquery-filtered shape reports audience removals as `move-out` events over row tags, and that the device cache tracks tags to apply them.

### F212 — device-store-browser tests the retired local-first stack

**Found while:** closing Stage 6, when the slow lane could not go green: `browser-suites (device-store-browser)` has failed on every `main` run since the Stage 4 merge (for example runs 35580319641 and 35582423261) and on `stage-6-sync`.

**What it is.** The suite (`services/api/playwright.device-store.config.ts` and its specs, run through `pnpm test:device-store-browser`) exercises the device store, the Rust sync-engine relay and the sealed store, all of which F199 retired. Nothing in the new architecture can make it pass.

**Ruling (founder, 21 September 2026).** Disable the job now, without recording an exception and without merging on a red check: `device-store-browser: device-store-browser tests the retired local-first stack; disabled at Stage 6, deleted with the stack in Stage 7.` It is a stand-alone job in `slow-lane.yml` with `if: false`, no longer a row of the `browser-suites` matrix.

**What is lost while it is disabled.** The suite also carried the accessibility smoke pass (`a11y-smoke.spec.ts`). That coverage is not replaced yet; whichever stage next needs an accessibility gate must rebuild it against the new stack rather than revive this job.

**Stage 7 obligation.** Delete, together with the retired code: the `device-store-browser` job in `.github/workflows/slow-lane.yml`, `services/api/playwright.device-store.config.ts`, the device-store browser suite and its `test:device-store-browser` script, and the `FDN-93` exclusion note, which has no meaning once the suite is gone.

### F213 — `member.projection_state` is vestigial

**Found while:** Stage 7, making workspace creation, removal and role change single server transactions.

The column and its check constraint (`pending`, `confirmed`, `revocation-pending`) carried the state of a two-phase protocol in which a device confirmed each membership change in its local graph. That protocol is gone. A new membership is `pending` only for the instant between the two steps of creation, removal now ends in `revoked/confirmed`, and `revocation-pending` is never written. Authorization queries still filter on `confirmed`, so the column is harmless, but it names a state machine that no longer exists.

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 22 September 2026:** the column and its check constraint stay; the column is dead in practice, not in the schema. Revisit during the FDN-104 spec sweep, not before. (Removing it means changing Better Auth's `member` additional fields and every query that reads it (the session guard, the principal resolver, the audience materializer, the shape proxy). That is a schema change to the authentication model and is left for that sweep.)

### F214 — the locked shell has no store left to lock

Raised 22 September 2026, correcting `VPS-D004` for Stage 8. **Ruled 22 September 2026 (founder-delegated decision; see the Ruling below).** It was raised as a design question, not a wording fix.

**What the section says.** The shell renders a full-bleed *Locked* screen, before anything mounts, whenever the sealed local store has not been unlocked by a server-authorized online unlock in this process (cold start), or has been sealed again by an authoritative denial from the role-refresh checkpoint (mid-session, F146). Only an authoritative denial locks; a timeout, a 5xx or an unreachable network does not (F148). A denied revocation and an unreachable server render identically (non-enumeration), and the client may distinguish them to decide whether to seal the store while still rendering both the same. *Locked* and *Offline* are different: an unlocked offline device never shows Locked.

**What no longer exists.** The sealed store, `POST /device-store/unlock`, the role-refresh checkpoint and the worker that held the live store are all deleted. A device now opens its cache from identifiers alone (`session_hint`), with no unlock and no token, and boots offline straight into `Offline`. There is nothing that can be sealed, so neither variant can occur, and the two properties the section protected have no implementation to hang on: (1) *a person whose access was withdrawn must not be shown their workspace*, and (2) *what the client shows must not reveal why*.

**What the current architecture already does.** The server refuses every request from a session that is not an active member of the workspace with the same non-distinguishing failure (`requireCurrentWorkspaceSession` raises one error for no session, wrong workspace and revoked membership; tRPC answers `401`). The shape proxy answers every way of not being an active member with one byte-identical `access-revoked` body and `erase: true`, and the client's response to that is to erase that workspace's cache (Stage 6). An unreachable server is a network error, not a `401`, and leaves the shell as it is.

**Proposed replacement (for a ruling).** One state, replacing both variants:

- **Trigger.** Any `401`/`UNAUTHORIZED` from an authenticated call, or an `access-revoked` from the shape proxy. Never a network failure, a timeout, a 5xx or a rate limit; those keep the shell exactly as it is and show the ordinary `Offline` or error treatment.
- **Rendering.** The full-bleed treatment as today, no illustration, one `body` sentence and a `primary` **Retry**. Copy that names neither cause nor person, for example *"Reconnect to continue. Vulto needs to verify your session before opening your workspace."* The same copy for cold start and mid-session; there is no longer a difference in what was on screen that a store needs to protect.
- **What Retry does.** Re-issues the failed call. If it succeeds the shell mounts; if it fails the same way the same state remains. It never says why.
- **Data.** On `access-revoked` the client has already erased that workspace's cache, so nothing readable is behind the screen. On a plain `401` (an expired or missing session) nothing was erased and the cache stays, but it is not rendered until a call succeeds.
- **Kept.** *Locked* is still distinct from *Offline*: an already-open device that loses connectivity keeps its cache and shows `Offline`. Only an authoritative refusal produces this state (F148). No feature may reuse the treatment.
- **Removed.** The unlock step, the cold-start/mid-session split, and "the local store stays sealed"; the unsaved-work clause becomes the outbox's (queued Tier 0 changes are kept until sent, and are erased only by a revocation or sign-out, per Stage 6).

**Tradeoffs to rule on.**
1. *A person whose session simply expired sees the same screen as a person removed from the workspace.* That is what non-enumeration asks for, but it is a worse experience for the common case (an expired session). The alternative is to send a plainly unauthenticated response (no session at all) to sign-in, and reserve the *Reconnect* state for a session that exists but is refused. That is safe today, because the proxy already distinguishes `unauthenticated` from `access-revoked`, and a missing session leaks nothing about any workspace. It does add a second refusal treatment, which the section currently forbids.
2. *A removed person sees a screen that says "Reconnect" and keeps offering Retry forever.* They cannot be told, and there is nowhere for a Retry to succeed. Accepting that is the price of the property. The alternative (a terminal state after N identical refusals) would itself reveal the difference between an outage and a refusal.
3. *`access-revoked` erases that workspace's cache.* That is a device-visible signal the client necessarily acts on, so the client does distinguish; what it must not do is render the distinction. The section's own last paragraph already says this ("non-enumeration governs what is rendered, never what the client concludes").
4. *Nothing is sealed on a plain `401`.* Cached Tier 0 rows remain on the device until a call succeeds or the person signs out. The old design sealed them. If that residual exposure matters (a shared computer, a session revoked while the laptop is closed), the alternative is to erase the workspace's cache on any `401`, at the cost of destroying a returning user's cache on every expiry.

**Recommendation.** The single state above, with tradeoff 1 decided in favor of sending a plainly missing session to sign-in and everything else to *Reconnect*, and tradeoff 4 left as stated (no erase on a plain `401`). This is a recommendation only; the section stays as it is, marked, until it is ruled.

**Consequence of leaving it open.** Nothing in Stage 8 renders a shell state; the marker keeps the historical section from being built to.

**Ruling (22 September 2026).** The proposed replacement is accepted as written: one *Reconnect* state, triggered only by `401`/`UNAUTHORIZED` on an authenticated call or `access-revoked` from the shape proxy, never by a network failure, timeout, 5xx or rate limit; full-bleed, one **Retry** that re-issues the failed call and never says why. **Tradeoff 1** is decided as recommended: a call carrying no session at all (never authenticated on this device, or already signed out) goes to sign-in; *Reconnect* is reserved for a call that had a session and was refused. Non-enumeration holds, because within "had a session, got refused" an expired and a revoked session still render identically, and whether a device ever had a session says nothing about any workspace or person. **Tradeoff 4** is decided as recommended: nothing is erased on a plain `401`; cached rows stay until a call succeeds or the person signs out. That is accepted only because the device cache holds Tier 0 only by the tier system's design, so a lapsed session on a shared device exposes nothing beyond what that design already decided is safe to leave there. **If a future stage widens what the device cache holds, this decision must be revisited.** Tradeoffs 2 and 3 are accepted as stated.

`VPS-D004` is corrected to match (the "Locked shell" section is now "The reconnect state", the marker is removed and the Decisions Recorded entry records this ruling). **Building the state (the component, its `401`/`access-revoked` wiring and its tests) is not part of this closure; it is tracked as Stage 9.**

### F215 — the identity link had three consumers that could not use it

Raised 22 September 2026, building RST-33. The Stage 8 brief expected `resolveEmployeeForUser` to be the only change (both of its callers already treat `null` as "cannot tell"). Building it showed the link had more consumers than that, and none of them could act on it.

1. **Row scopes.** `rowScopeSatisfied(scope)` in the interceptor was a stub for the one seam its own comment said "changes when the link lands": it was true only for `any`. So a Manager's `direct-reports` grant, a team member's `own-plus-team` read and `own` compensation read all resolved to no access, and the derived Manager role widened nothing. Implemented for an Employee row, from the real link and the real `managed_by` edges: `own` (the row is the caller's Employee), `direct-reports` (the caller is the row's active manager) and `own-plus-team` (`own`, or the row shares the caller's active manager). Every other scope, and every row that is not an Employee, is still unsatisfied, because they need a subject path this does not follow. Edge writes still name no row, so a Manager cannot move people; whoever may write an Employee's operational half at role level (Owner, HR Admin) may.
2. **Reader-set resolution.** `resolveReaderSet` returned `unresolvable` for any row-dependent grant, regardless of the link. Employee compensation (Tier 1) has a team-member `own` read, so Gate 3 ("a protected record must have someone who can read it") could never pass and no compensation could be written by anyone. `own` is now resolved from the subject: the reader it adds is the person the record concerns, if they hold the role and have a login. A derived Manager, and every other row-dependent scope, remain unresolvable.
3. **Subject exclusion on reads.** A004-T16 requires the exclusion "at the interceptor, in the materialized sync audience and in `protected.read`", but it was implemented only as a filter inside the write gate's reader-set. It is now applied in `decideRead`, the one decision all three consume, returning `restricted` with the label from `VPS-D004` (*"Restricted — this record concerns you."*).

**Decisions inside those, for the reviewer to confirm.** (a) A reader with no Employee record is treated as not being the subject, because "the subject" is an Employee record and a login with none is not one; the alternative (deny anyone whose link cannot be determined) would hide every HR case from an Owner who never created their own Employee record. (b) A case with no recorded subject is not excluded for anyone (the write gate already refuses a case with no resolvable subject, so this is a data-inconsistency corner). (c) `own-plus-team` means "shares my active manager"; the spec says "own plus team" without defining team.

**Cost.** Each read decision for an Employee row now resolves the caller's Employee and, for the manager-based scopes, one or two edge lookups. Acceptable at 150 rows a directory; a candidate for a per-request cache if it is not.

### F216 — the Reconnect state had no session-aware shell call to retry

Raised 22 September 2026 by the Stage 9 builder while tracing the Reconnect brief before implementation, and correctly stopped on rather than resolved unilaterally, per the project's decision policy. Verified directly against the code, not taken on the report's word: `apps/roster-web/src/app/(shell)/layout.tsx` wraps only the fixture `Shell.tsx`, which hardcodes `workspaceName="Northgate Studio"` and `syncStatus="Synced"` as literals — not state, not a query, not a prop threaded from anywhere real. `createGraphClient` is mounted nowhere in the application except the standalone `/sync-harness` diagnostic route. `authClient.useSession()` appears only on `/auth-ready` and `/devices`, both outside the shell; `/auth-ready`'s own copy says plainly that "workspace access remains closed until the server confirms the membership projection." Every screen under `(shell)/` — Home, People, Timesheets, Foundations — renders from hardcoded fixture data (`EMPLOYEES`, a fixed viewer, a client-side `canSeeCompensation()` helper unrelated to the real interceptor). `SyncEngine.#handleRevoked` stops every source and publishes `signedOut: true` before any retry could reuse that worker, confirming the report's claim that Retry cannot be wired as a literal re-issue once `access-revoked` fires.

**This is wider than Stage 9.** It is `FDN-69` — "Deliver the shared application shell and end-to-end workspace bootstrap" — never built, sitting in Backlog since before Stage 1. Nothing in `apps/roster-web` has ever been connected to the real backend Stages 1–8 built; the whole front end is still the original design prototype. Reconnect cannot be built against a call that does not exist.

**Ruling: the contract, checked directly against what already exists rather than invented from scratch.** `services/api/src/auth/config.ts` already configures Better Auth's `organization()` plugin server-side; the session already carries `activeOrganizationId`, and `services/api/src/auth/workspace-session.ts` already clears it to `null` on revocation — `requireCurrentWorkspaceSession` already treats it as the workspace claim. The gap is client-side only: `apps/roster-web/src/lib/auth-client.ts` has no `organizationClient()` plugin, so nothing in the shell can read what the server already tracks.

1. No Better Auth session (client-side, no workspace call needed) → sign-in.
2. A session → add `organizationClient()`, read `user.id` and `session.activeOrganizationId`, construct the real graph client with those two values (the same constructor `/sync-harness` already exercises), and mount it in the shell in place of the fixture props.
3. Offline cold start with a cached `session_hint` mounts from the cache with no network call, per `VPS-A003`'s restored offline boot — this must not regress.
4. A `401`/`access-revoked` from the mounted client is surfaced to the shell as a signal; rendering it is Reconnect's job, not this stage's.
5. Retry, for Reconnect, means re-running this bootstrap from scratch — there is nothing to retry inside a client `#handleRevoked` already stopped.

**Scope, deliberately minimal.** Only the shell's own bootstrap becomes real. Home, People, Timesheets and the rest stay on fixtures; migrating them is separate, larger work, not folded into this ruling. Full text of the build brief: `docs/Claude_Code_Build_Prompt.md`, "### Stage 9 — the real shell bootstrap." The original Stage 9 (Reconnect) brief is unchanged in substance and renumbered Stage 10, now unblocked once Stage 9 lands.

### F217 — the ruled shell bootstrap had no authoritative active workspace to open

Raised 22 September 2026 by the Stage 9 builder (Codex, fresh session, `stage-9-shell-bootstrap`) while tracing F216's ruling against the live workspace lifecycle before writing code — another correct stop, not a routing-around. Verified directly: `services/api/src/auth/workspace-projection.ts`'s `createWorkspace` calls `createPendingWorkspaceAdmission` then `confirmWorkspaceAdmission` and returns `{ workspaceId, membershipId }`; neither function touches `session.activeOrganizationId`. A repository-wide search for writes to that field finds exactly one: `workspace-session.ts`'s revocation path, which sets it to `null`. The existing workspace-creation test calls `requireCurrentWorkspaceSession` with the new workspace id passed explicitly, never asserting the session's active organization was set — because it never is. Offline, `packages/graph/src/sync-client/database.ts` names each cache `vulto:<workspaceId>:<userId>`; `session_hint` inside one repeats that pair but nothing outside it records which cache was last opened. `device-identity.ts`'s `vulto:device` database is deliberately narrow — `assertAllowedKey` and a pinned test permit only a device id and a pending-erase list, never workspace or person state — so there is nowhere to add an active-workspace pointer without a real, reviewed contract change.

F216's ruling was incomplete on this point: it confirmed the *field* existed and is read correctly, not that anything *populates* it. That gap is on this review, not on the builder catching it.

**Ruling.** `confirmWorkspaceAdmission` (`services/api/src/auth/workspace-session.ts`) sets `session.activeOrganizationId` to the newly confirmed workspace, in the same transaction, **only when that membership is the person's sole active one** — checked the same way `revokeWorkspaceAdmission` already checks membership state, not invented fresh. A person who creates or joins a second workspace while already active in a first is **not** auto-switched; their current active organization is left alone. This covers the ordinary case (a founding Owner's first workspace, or anyone's first membership) without silently deciding workspace switching, which `docs/Reviewer_Handoff.md` already lists as undesigned and deferred.

A session with a **null** active organization is resolved by the shell bootstrap itself: if the person has exactly one active membership, open it (there is no real ambiguity, whatever cleared the field); if they have more than one, or none, the shell cannot pick, and must not guess — render whatever the minimal not-yet-a-workspace-selector state is for Stage 9 (a plain "reconnect to choose a workspace" holding state is enough; it is not F214's `Reconnect` and must not reuse that trigger or copy), not a silently wrong workspace.

Offline, the same rule: **exactly one cached workspace for the signed-in user boots from it; more than one is unresolved and requires coming online**, because there is no persisted marker to pick from and adding one to the device-identity database is a scope change this ruling does not make. This is a recorded limitation, not a defect — the same one workspace switching already carries. No change to `device-identity.ts`'s allowed-key list.

**Scope check.** This does not touch `Offline`, `Reconnect` (Stage 10), or any fixture screen. It adds one narrow write inside an existing transaction and one read-time rule at the shell boundary. `F217` closed.


### F218 — cold offline boot conflicts with the unconditional no-session redirect

Raised 22 September 2026 by the third Stage 9 builder on `stage-9-shell-bootstrap-v2`, before implementation. The revised Stage 9 brief says that `authClient.useSession()` resolving with no data once `isPending` clears is unconditionally “sign in,” and that this is checked first with zero workspace calls. The same brief requires a fresh offline load to enumerate `session_hint` caches and mount when exactly one eligible workspace exists.

The pinned Better Auth 1.6.29 client initializes its session atom with `data: null` and `isPending: true` (`dist/client/session-atom.mjs`). When `/get-session` fails on a cold offline load, its catch path leaves `data: null`, records the error, and sets `isPending: false` (lines 94–103). Thus the specified redirect condition is true for the required offline-boot case. A real no-session response and a network failure differ by the error field, but the brief expressly says no data after pending clears unconditionally redirects. Making an exception for errors, or selecting an IndexedDB identity when the live session is unavailable, is a behavioral and security decision rather than a private implementation detail. `session_hint` contains workspace and user IDs, but it is not an authenticated session.

**Ruling — closed 22 September 2026.** Checked directly against the pinned client, not just the report. `session-atom.mjs` has two distinct error paths, not one. When `$fetch` returns normally (the request reached the server, whatever it answered — a clean no-session response, a 401, a 5xx), `error` is either `null` or a `BetterFetchError` (`@better-fetch/fetch`'s own error class, which always carries a numeric `status`). When the fetch itself throws before any response — network unreachable — the `catch (fetchError)` branch sets `data: latest.data` (`null` on a cold boot) and `error: fetchError`, whatever the underlying fetch implementation threw (a `TypeError`, an abort `DOMException`); this is never a `BetterFetchError` and never carries a numeric `.status`. The two are reliably distinguishable: `error` present without a numeric `.status` means the server was never reached. This mirrors a split the sync engine already makes for the identical reason — `packages/graph/src/sync-client/engine.ts`'s `#onFailure` treats `failure.kind === "network"` (offline; retried with backoff) differently from `"unauthenticated"` (session lapsed; no retry) — so the fix reuses an established shape rather than inventing one.

The sign-in redirect (Stage 9 point 1) now fires only on a confirmed answer: `data` null and `error` either `null` or carrying a numeric `.status`. An `error` present without one is indeterminate, not a confirmed absence of session, and falls through to the offline cache-boot path (point 4) instead. Point 4 needed one addition the original wording didn't anticipate: when point 1 couldn't resolve at all, there is no `user.id` to filter cached workspaces by (Better Auth never answered), so the shell enumerates every `vulto:<workspaceId>:<userId>` cache on the device via `listIdbDatabases()`, unfiltered — exactly one across the whole device mounts from its `session_hint`; more than one, whether that is one person's two workspaces or two people sharing the device, is the same unresolved-selection case F217 already established and requires coming online. No new identity mechanism is introduced, and `device-identity.ts`'s deliberately narrow allowed-key list is not reopened. `apps/roster-web/src/app/(shell)/` (Part 3, Stage 9, points 1 and 4) and this file are updated in the same pass; `VPS-A003` and `VPS-F001` need no wording change — both already describe the offline-boot and sign-in behaviors this ruling reconciles, not new ones.

### F219 — the null-active-organization fallback has no permitted membership read

Raised 22 September 2026 during the fourth Stage 9 attempt (`stage-9-shell-bootstrap-v3`, FDN-117), after implementing the ruled bootstrap and exercising it in a real browser. Stage 9 point 3 says to add Better Auth's `organizationClient()` and, when a valid session has `activeOrganizationId: null`, resolve exactly one active membership on the client. The client plugin makes `organization.list()` callable, but `services/api/src/auth/http.ts` explicitly returns 404 for **every** `/api/auth/organization/*` path. Its comment explains why: the plugin's public routes are kept closed so that organization mutation and invitation commands have no second write path beside FDN-85 and FDN-86. The route guard covers read calls too.

**Proof.** The browser test `a null active organization resolves a sole confirmed membership` creates and confirms one workspace, clears only `session.activeOrganizationId`, and loads the shell with a valid session. Its trace shows `GET /api/auth/get-session` succeeded, then `GET /api/auth/organization/list` returned 404. No `list-members` request follows; the shell displays the unresolved holding state for a person who has exactly one active membership. A separate test with two memberships correctly holds. This is a failure of the required single-membership case, not an offline condition or a fixture.

**Ruling — closed 22 September 2026.** Checked directly: `services/api/src/trpc.ts`'s `createContext` reads `session.activeOrganizationId` and returns `principal: null` when it's absent — meaning every `protectedProcedure`, `principal.current` included, is unreachable without an already-resolved workspace. There is no existing endpoint anywhere in the API that answers "which workspaces is this session an active member of" without already knowing the answer. Opening Better Auth's `organization/list` would not by itself be safe either: `member.status` is `'pending' | 'active' | 'revoked'` directly on the row Better Auth's own plugin reads, so an unfiltered list would surface pending and revoked memberships the shell must never silently pick among (F217).

No `/api/auth/organization/*` route opens — FDN-85/FDN-86's boundary is correct as built and is not reopened for reads either. Instead, one new narrow route: `POST /workspace/list-active-memberships` in `services/api/src/auth/http.ts`, requiring only a valid Better Auth session (no workspace header, no workspace scope needed — the same shape as the existing `/devices/retire`, whose own comment already establishes this pattern: "takes no workspaceId, because it has no workspace scope"). It runs the exact predicate already used twice in this file — `requireCurrentWorkspaceSession`'s join conditions and `confirmWorkspaceAdmission`'s post-confirmation active-membership count: `member.userId = session.user.id`, `member.status = "active"`, `member.projectionState = "confirmed"`, `organization.status = "active"` — and returns `{ workspaceId, workspaceName }` for each match, nothing else. A person with no active confirmed membership (never admitted, or only pending) gets an empty array, which is the same "none" branch point 3 already defines as unresolved — not a new case.

Stage 9 point 3 is corrected: drop `organizationClient()` from `auth-client.ts` entirely (there is nothing left for it to legitimately call), and `soleActiveWorkspace` (the preliminary code's own name for this resolver) calls the new route instead of `organization.list()`/`organization.listMembers()`. Every other piece of the preliminary work on `stage-9-shell-bootstrap-v3` — the F218 `hasServerAnswer` split, the offline cached-workspace fallback, the graph client construction, the refusal-signal wiring in `Shell.tsx`/`layout.tsx`, and the `packages/graph/src/sync-client/*` changes — is correct and untouched by this finding; none of it should be discarded. `VPS-F001`'s "Workspace admission control" section is corrected with one added bullet recording this boundary and the one exception, so a future reader does not have to rediscover it from the route guard's code comment.

**Status: closed.** Continue Stage 9 on the same branch, same session — this is a surgical fix to already-good work, not a restart.

### F220 — resolving the sole membership does not establish an active session workspace

Raised 22 September 2026 while continuing Stage 9 on `stage-9-shell-bootstrap-v3` after F219 was ruled. The F219 endpoint is implemented as specified: a valid session with `activeOrganizationId: null` gets an array containing its one active confirmed workspace, and the server integration test excludes pending, revoked, suspended and other-user rows. The shell then takes that `workspaceId` as selected and constructs `createGraphClient`.

**Proof from the browser and guards.** The previously failing browser test again shows the workspace name never appears, although its trace now records `POST /workspace/list-active-memberships` returning HTTP 200 with exactly one `{ workspaceId, workspaceName }`. The page mounts a shell but remains `Offline` with the generic "Workspace" label. `services/api/src/auth/http.ts`'s `/devices/register` handler rejects the client's `x-vulto-workspace-id` with 403 when it differs from `session.activeOrganizationId`; null necessarily differs. The sync engine catches device-registration failure, but the shape proxy's `accessRevoked` requires a registered device and refuses the shape. `services/api/src/trpc.ts` also returns `principal: null` whenever `activeOrganizationId` is null, so named mutations and protected reads cannot work in the resolved workspace. This is not a data-filtering failure in the new list route; it is a missing transition from a client-side selection to the existing server-authoritative session claim.

**Root cause, checked directly.** `confirmWorkspaceAdmission` (F217's fix) is the only place in production code that ever sets `session.activeOrganizationId` for the ordinary sole-membership case, and it fires exactly once, at confirmation, updating every session row that person holds *at that moment* (`.where(eq(session.userId, confirmed.userId))`, no `activeOrganizationId` filter — safe because it only runs once sole active membership is independently proven). It cannot reach a session created afterward: a fresh sign-in on a new device or browser, for someone already an active confirmed member, creates a new `session` row with `activeOrganizationId` at its column default (`null`, `services/api/src/auth/schema.ts`), and nothing sets it going forward. `services/api/src/auth/config.ts`'s `organization()` plugin config has no auto-activate behavior either. So the "null active organization, exactly one active membership" case Stage 9 point 3 was written for is not a rare edge case — it is the ordinary state of every fresh sign-in for an already-confirmed single-workspace person, and nothing before F220 ever gave that state a way forward.

**Ruling — closed 22 September 2026.** A second new route, `POST /workspace/activate-sole-membership`, added beside F219's route in `services/api/src/auth/http.ts`: session-gated, no request body. It re-derives the caller's active/confirmed memberships itself, from the session alone — it does not trust or accept a client-supplied `workspaceId`, the same discipline `trpc.ts`'s own context-building already follows ("nothing the client sends... can name or alter" the principal). When exactly one exists, it performs the identical write `confirmWorkspaceAdmission` already performs — `update(session).set({ activeOrganizationId })` filtered by that user's id, covering every session row they currently hold — and returns `{ workspaceId }`. When zero or more than one exist, it writes nothing and returns `{ workspaceId: null }`; this is not an error, it is the same "none/many is unresolved" state point 3 already defines, surfaced through this route rather than invented twice. This neither reopens Better Auth's organization plugin (F219's boundary is untouched) nor builds workspace switching (an already-set `activeOrganizationId` is never the target of this write — the route only ever establishes, from null, what independent server-side proof already shows the person's sole active workspace to be).

Stage 9 point 3 is corrected: after the shell resolves a candidate workspace (from `session.activeOrganizationId` directly, or via F219's list route when it was null), it calls this new activation route before constructing the graph client, and only proceeds when the response confirms the same workspace — a stale race where the answer changed underneath falls into the same holding state as "none/many," not a crash. `VPS-F001`'s "Workspace admission control" section gets one more clause naming both new routes together as the session's active-organization boundary.

**Status: closed.** Continue on `stage-9-shell-bootstrap-v3` — the F219 work and existing preliminary bootstrap are unaffected and stay exactly as they are.

### F221 — Entity transfer does not fit the Silent Audit Log's closed taxonomy

Raised 22 September 2026 while briefing Stage 11 (`VRS-F003`, Multi-Entity and Jurisdiction Foundation), the next MVP-order feature after RST-33. `VRS-F003`'s Security Considerations states plainly: "Entity transfer is an audited event per `VPS-F004`, because it changes which employment law governs a person." Checked directly against `VPS-F004`'s own schema: `event_type` is a closed enum of exactly five values — `PermissionDenied`, `SensitiveAccessGranted`, `AuthorizedOperationFailed`, `PrivilegedProjectionAuthorized`, `CryptographicErasureExecuted` — enforced by a database check constraint (`services/api/src/audit/schema.ts`), and every existing writer of `AuditEntry` (`services/api/src/permission/interceptor.ts`'s automatic Tier 1/3 access and denial logging, `services/api/src/protected/erasure.ts`'s key-destruction event) uses one of those five. An Entity transfer is an ordinary, permitted, Owner/HR-Admin-initiated write of Tier 0 data — not a denial, not a sensitive-tier access grant, not an operation failure, not a privileged projection, and not a cryptographic erasure. None of the five categories describes it, and `VPS-F004`'s own body (its screen, its filter set, its acceptance criteria) confirms the log's whole design is permission- and access-focused, not a general business-activity trail.

**Ruling — closed 22 September 2026.** The underlying need — "who moved this employee to which entity, and when, so it can be questioned after the fact" — is already met structurally and does not need the Silent Audit Log at all. Every `scoped_to_entity` edge carries `created_by`, `created_at`, `effective_from` and `effective_to` per `VPS-A002`'s Universal Edge Conventions, and G02/G03 already require the full transfer history to be preserved and traversable. That is a complete, permanent, queryable record of every transfer, its actor and its timing — exactly what the spec's stated reason for wanting an audit trail asks for. Extending `VPS-F004`'s closed taxonomy for one feature's ordinary write would set a precedent this project has deliberately avoided elsewhere (cf. F206's system-operation additions, which were all genuinely access- or erasure-related); it is also unnecessary. `VRS-F003`'s Security Considerations bullet is corrected in place: Entity transfer is recorded through the edge's own history, not through `VPS-F004`.

**Status: closed.** No `VPS-F004` change, no migration, no new audit call in `employee.setEntity`.

### F222 — no path creates a workspace's first Entity

Raised 22 September 2026 during the same Stage 11 brief. `VRS-F003`'s G04 states "every workspace has at least one Entity. It cannot be reduced to zero," and its own body says the first Entity is "created automatically during `VPS-F006`'s setup wizard... from the founder's own answers." Checked directly: `VPS-F006` (Workspace Setup and Data Import) has no implementation anywhere in `apps/` or `services/` — there is no setup-wizard route or Entity-creating code path in `services/api/src/auth/workspace-projection.ts` (the module that creates a workspace). `services/api/src/graph/founding.ts`'s `writeFoundingRecords` writes exactly five records on workspace creation — Workspace, the founding User, WorkspaceMembership, and the two membership edges — and no Entity. This is not merely `VRS-F003`'s own gap: `VRS-F002`'s already-built `employeeCreate` mutation (`services/api/src/mutations/employee.ts`) requires a valid, existing `entity_id` and rejects with `invalid-args` when the referenced node is missing or not an `Entity` — so a freshly created workspace, today, cannot create its first Employee either, since it has no Entity to scope one to and no way to make one.

**Ruling — closed 22 September 2026.** Rather than wait for `VPS-F006` (not briefed, no target stage), `writeFoundingRecords` gains a sixth founding record, written in the same transaction as the other five: a default Entity named after the workspace's own name (the same string `createWorkspace` already receives and uses for the `Workspace` node — no new input is asked of anyone), `jurisdiction: "Global"` (the enum's existing catch-all for "no specific employment jurisdiction," never asserting a jurisdiction nobody has confirmed), `default_currency: "USD"` (the most neutral available placeholder; Vulto's stated markets have no single obvious default), `lifecycle_status: "Active"`. All three fields are editable afterward through `entity.update` by an Owner or HR Admin, including before any Employee is created. When `VPS-F006` is eventually built, its setup wizard may ask the founder's own questions and correct these defaults in place — this ruling is the interim bootstrap that keeps G04 true starting now, not a substitute for that wizard. `VRS-F003` is corrected to record this bootstrap path explicitly, since its prior text assumed `VPS-F006` already existed.

**Status: closed.** `services/api/src/graph/founding.ts` and its callers are Stage 11's to update; no `VPS-F006` work is implied or required.

### F223 — Entity deactivation has no defined state transition or base version

Raised 22 September 2026 while tracing the Stage 11 brief before implementation (FDN-118). The brief instructs the builder to add `entity.deactivate(entityId)` and enforce the separate G04 and G05 refusals. `VRS-F003` gives exactly that API signature. Its Entity schema has both `is_active: boolean` and `lifecycle_status: Active | Dissolved`, but it does not define whether deactivation sets `is_active` to false, moves the lifecycle to `Dissolved`, or does both. Those choices lead to different definitions of the “remaining Active Entity” counted by G04, and different behavior for later reads and updates.

There is a separate contract conflict in the same operation. `VPS-A003`'s Conflict resolution section and A003-T54 require every state-transition mutation to carry the base `version` on which the decision was made, rejecting a changed version with `stale-state`. The Stage 4 brief implemented that rule for `graph.transitionLifecycle` through required `expected_version`, and Stage 8's `employee.transitionStatus` follows it. Deactivation is a change from active to inactive or dissolved, yet neither Stage 11's brief nor `VRS-F003` gives `entity.deactivate` a version argument or an exception to A003-T54. Marking it `stateTransition: false` or silently using the current database version would evade the existing concurrency rule; adding a required argument would change the stated API contract. Neither choice is authorized by the brief.

**Ruling — closed 22 September 2026.** `is_active` is dropped from Entity's schema. It was never anything but a leftover: every other `fixedLifecycle`-registered node type (`WorkingCalendar`, `Holiday`, `WorkingPattern`, all owned by `VRS-F004`) carries `lifecycle_status` alone in its own schema block, with no separate boolean — confirmed directly against their spec text and against `packages/schema/src/registry/nodes.ts`. `is_active` is the convention `featureOwnedLifecycle` node types use instead (`VRS-F018`'s LeavePolicy, `VRS-F006`'s RateCard, `VPS-F010`'s CustomFieldDefinition — each registered `lifecycle: featureOwnedLifecycle` and each carries `is_active` with no `lifecycle_status` field of its own). Entity is registered `lifecycle: fixedLifecycle("Active", "Dissolved")` — the platform-fixed pattern, not the feature-owned one — so its spec text carrying both fields was simply never cleaned up when it was standardized onto `fixedLifecycle`. `VRS-F003`'s schema is corrected to drop `is_active` entirely; deactivation transitions `lifecycle_status` from `Active` to `Dissolved`, and G04/G05's "remaining Active Entity" / "Active scoped employees" language refers to `lifecycle_status = "Active"`, nothing else.

The version gap is resolved the same way `VRS-F002` resolved it for Employee, not by inventing a new rule. `graph.transitionLifecycle` already implements A003-T54 generically for any `fixedLifecycle` node — it writes `lifecycle_status` through `updateNodeFields`'s own `expected_version` check — but it refuses any node type in `FEATURE_LIFECYCLE_NODE_TYPES` (currently only `Employee`) precisely because a feature-specific transition needs business-rule checks the generic mutation cannot express. `entity.deactivate` needs exactly that: G04 and G05 are business rules, not a bare state flip. `Entity` joins `FEATURE_LIFECYCLE_NODE_TYPES` alongside `Employee`, and `entity.deactivate`'s API contract gains the argument its sibling `employee.transitionStatus` already has: `entity.deactivate(entityId, expectedVersion) -> { success }`. Its `validate()` checks `expected_version` against the current row first (`stale-state`, A003-T54, exactly `employeeTransitionStatus`'s own first check), then G04 and G05 as the two distinct business refusals already briefed; `apply()` writes `lifecycle_status: "Dissolved"` through `updateNodeFields` under the checked version, the same call `transitionLifecycle` and `employeeTransitionStatus` both make. `VRS-F003` and the Stage 11 brief are both corrected to match.

**Status: closed.** `packages/schema/src/mutations/employee.ts`'s `FEATURE_LIFECYCLE_NODE_TYPES` gains `"Entity"`; the new `packages/schema/src/mutations/entity.ts` and its `services/api` implementation carry the corrected signature from the start. Stage 11 is unblocked.

### F224 — Stage 11's shared Tier 0 registry needs an offline Entity transfer path

Raised 22 September 2026 while implementing Stage 11 on `codex/stage-11-multi-entity-jurisdiction`, after F223 was ruled. The Stage 11 brief calls this a server-side-only stage and says `entity.resolveForEmployee` has one implementation in `services/api`. Its G06 check says nothing else in the stage may traverse `scoped_to_entity` directly. That is enough for server reads: the new `services/api/src/graph/entity-resolution.ts` can own the traversal, and the server mutations can call it.

The mutation names live in `packages/schema/src/mutations/foundation.ts`'s shared `MUTATIONS` registry, not a server-only registry. `packages/graph/src/mutators/foundation.ts` declares `OPTIMISTIC_MUTATORS: Readonly<Record<MutationName, OptimisticMutator>>`; after adding `entity.create`, `entity.update`, `entity.deactivate`, and `employee.setEntity`, `pnpm verify` reaches TypeScript and fails because all four client handlers are missing (TS2739).
