This is a required boundary, not a cosmetic type error: `VPS-A003`'s Decision says Tier 0 mutations apply to the local cache immediately, and A003-T63 requires Tier 0 writes to queue offline. A placeholder handler returning no cache changes would compile but break the promised optimistic behavior.

`employee.setEntity` makes the unresolved choice concrete. Its optimistic half would have to close the existing open `scoped_to_entity` edge and insert the new one in the device cache. The current edge ID is not an input to the mutation and cannot be inferred from `employeeId`; the client must inspect cached edges to find it. Doing that in a new `packages/graph` handler introduces a direct `scoped_to_entity` traversal outside `services/api`'s sole resolver, contrary to Stage 11 point 3 and its report check. Calling the server resolver is impossible while offline. Moving the resolution logic into a shared package so server and device use one implementation, or changing the offline contract, would alter the brief and needs a ruling.

**Ruling — closed 22 September 2026.** G06 protects one thing: *temporal resolution* — computing which Entity governed an employee as of a given moment, the `asOf`-parameterized question `entity.resolveForEmployee` answers and that contract generation, leave and payroll must never re-derive themselves. It does not extend to the mechanical, non-temporal question a single-active-edge-with-history mutation must answer about its own state before it can act: *which edge is currently open*. `services/api/src/graph/entity-resolution.ts`'s own `resolveEntityAssignment(tx, ws, employeeId, at: string | "open")` already keeps these two questions distinct inside the server — `at: "open"` never interprets an `asOf` date, it returns the one edge G02's single-active-edge-with-history invariant guarantees is unique. The client optimistic handlers only ever need that same `"open"` question, never the `asOf` one, so G06 is not engaged.

That pattern is already built and already shipping: `org.moveEmployee`'s existing optimistic mutator (`packages/graph/src/mutators/foundation.ts`) finds the employee's currently-open `managed_by` edge by querying `c.cache.edgesFrom(employeeId, "managed_by")` for `effectiveTo === null` directly in the device cache, with no resolver of its own and no `VRS-F003`-style G06 restriction on `managed_by` — because, exactly as here, a bare "find the open edge" query has one correct answer by the same single-active-edge-with-history invariant and needs no `asOf` interpretation. `employee.setEntity`'s optimistic handler follows the identical shape for `scoped_to_entity`: find the one edge with `effectiveTo === null`, close it at `effective_from`, and open a new one to the destination Entity, deriving the new edge's id from `moveEmployeeEdgeId(mutationId)` — already the shared, portable, deterministic derivation `services/api/src/mutations/entity.ts`'s own server implementation uses for the same edge, so client and server independently compute the same id and replication finds nothing to reconcile. That `scoped_to_entity` is already client-cached and directly writable is not new: `employeeCreate`'s existing optimistic mutator already writes a `scoped_to_entity` edge into the device cache when an Employee is created.

`entity.create` and `entity.update` need nothing beyond ordinary node-write optimistic mutators, the same shape `employeeCreate`/`employeeUpdate` already use for a plain node with no lifecycle business rules of its own.

`entity.deactivate` is different, and is not given `employeeTransitionStatus`'s full client-side replication treatment. `employeeTransitionStatus`'s optimistic mutator can safely replicate its business rules offline because they are single-node rules, decidable from the one cached Employee record. G04 and G05 are not: they are workspace-wide aggregate counts (every other Entity's `lifecycle_status`, every Employee's current scope) that a single device's partial, possibly-stale cache cannot be trusted to answer correctly, and a wrong offline answer would let a deactivation appear to succeed on a device that is about to be told otherwise. `entity.deactivate`'s optimistic handler therefore checks only `expected_version` (`stale-state` on a mismatch, exactly `employeeTransitionStatus`'s own first check) and applies the `lifecycle_status: "Dissolved"` write optimistically; it does not re-check G04 or G05 client-side. If the server's authoritative check refuses a deactivation the client allowed through, that is exactly what `packages/graph/src/sync-client/outbox.ts`'s existing rejection path is for — `reject(mutationId, reason)` marks the entry `"rejected"` and its captured `undo: UndoEntry[]` reverses the optimistic write, the same general safety net every other optimistic mutation already relies on for a server-side refusal it can't predict offline. No new mechanism is introduced.

`VRS-F003`'s G06 is corrected to state the distinction explicitly, and its Decisions Recorded section gains this ruling.

**Status: closed.** `packages/graph/src/mutators/foundation.ts` gains four optimistic mutators: ordinary node writes for `entity.create`/`entity.update`; a `moveEmployee`-shaped close-and-reopen of the open `scoped_to_entity` edge for `employee.setEntity`, reusing `moveEmployeeEdgeId`; and a version-check-only write for `entity.deactivate`, with G04/G05 left to the server and the outbox's rejection path. Stage 11 is unblocked.

### F225 — Holiday confirmation is not, in fact, `VPS-F004`-audited

Raised 22 September 2026 while briefing Stage 12 (`VRS-F004`, Working Calendar and Working Patterns). `VRS-F004`'s Security Considerations states plainly that "Holiday confirmation is audited per `VPS-F004`, because it changes computed figures retroactively across the product." Checked directly against `VPS-F004_Silent_Audit_Log.md`'s own Technical Architecture: `AuditEntry.event_type` is a closed enum of exactly five values — `PermissionDenied`, `SensitiveAccessGranted`, `AuthorizedOperationFailed`, `PrivilegedProjectionAuthorized`, `CryptographicErasureExecuted` — and creation "happens exclusively within `VPS-A004`'s interceptor. No feature calls a create endpoint for this node type" (G02). An HR Admin confirming a provisional holiday's actual date is an ordinary, permitted Tier 0 write; it is not a denial, not a grant of Tier 1/Tier 3 access, not an operation failure, not a privileged projection and not an erasure. This is the identical shape of gap F221 already found and ruled for `VRS-F003`'s claim that Entity transfer is `VPS-F004`-audited.

**Ruling — closed 22 September 2026, on F221's own precedent.** `VPS-F004`'s taxonomy is not extended for holiday confirmation, for the same reason it was not extended for Entity transfer: the record already exists elsewhere. `Holiday`'s own schema carries `confirmed_at: timestamp, nullable` and `confirmed_by: user_id, nullable`, alongside the retained `estimated_date` that shows the shift — exactly the who/when/what-changed record an `AuditEntry` would otherwise supply, already present on the node itself with no additional write. The same reasoning covers `ReducedHoursPeriod`, which the spec gives an identical `is_provisional`/`estimated_start_date` confirmation shape but no dedicated audit fields of its own; since it is held inline on `WorkingCalendar` rather than as its own node (no independent lifecycle), its confirmation is likewise not a `VPS-F004` event — the superseding `WorkingCalendar` version's own `updated_at`/`updated_by` (per `VPS-A002`'s Universal Node Conventions) is its record. `VRS-F004`'s Security Considerations, its acceptance criterion for holiday confirmation, and its Decisions Recorded section are all corrected to match.

**Status: closed.** No `VPS-F004` `AuditEntry`-writing call is added anywhere in Stage 12's `holiday.confirm` or `calendar.update` implementations. Stage 12 is unblocked on this point before it starts.

### F226 — the working-day index's materialization is a later stage's problem, not this one's

Raised 22 September 2026 while briefing Stage 12, tracing `VRS-F004`'s Technical Architecture before writing the brief. The spec's "Materialization, and why it matters" section states the working-day index is "materialized in the SQLite-WASM index," rebuilt "in the worker," sized entirely against `VRS-F005`'s Bench Forecast rendering "150 employees across 90 days within 200ms" — a feature not yet built and with no scheduled stage. The three Non-Functional Requirements this drives (5ms/1ms read budgets, a 500ms incremental-rebuild budget "in the worker") are all specific to that index. Checked directly: nothing in `packages/graph/src/mutators/foundation.ts` or `packages/graph/src/sync-client` currently implements any per-employee-per-date materialized table, and no caller anywhere in the current codebase would exercise one at the volume that motivates it. The spec's own language — "materialized in the SQLite-WASM index," "in the worker" — describes the CRDT-era client-side materialization pipeline `VPS-A001` retired with the September 2026 server-authoritative pivot (Loro and its Worker-private materialization are gone; `wa-sqlite` is retained, but now "fed by replication rather than by CRDT materialization," per `VPS-A001`'s own corrected text). Building the index as originally specified, before its one consumer exists and before its own supporting architecture has been re-examined under the new model, risks building the wrong thing twice.

**Ruling — closed 22 September 2026, mirroring precedent already set twice in this project.** Stage 11's own brief deferred `entity.resolveForEmployee`'s real caller ("this stage has no real caller yet... prove it with tests") rather than build speculative consumers; Stage 8 and Stage 11 both deferred `apps/` UI work with no scheduled stage. The same judgment applies here, at larger scale: Stage 12 builds the complete, correct `WorkingCalendar`/`Holiday`/`WorkingPattern` data model and the full `workingDays.*` public API (G01–G05, G08), each function evaluating the spec's own resolution order directly against the graph on every call — no cache, no precomputed table, no index. This is correct, testable, and sufficient for everything Stage 12 itself needs to prove, including every one of the spec's acceptance criteria that does not depend on the index's specific performance numbers. G06 (the index's existence and materialization) and the index-rebuild half of G07 are deferred in full to whichever stage builds `VRS-F005` — the feature whose render budget is the only reason the index exists — where it will be designed against the server-authoritative architecture as it stands at that time, not against the retired one this document's prose still describes. G07's three recalculation exemptions (a closed PayRun, an approved LeaveRequest, a signed Contract) are **not** deferred: they are business rules about what recalculates, independent of whether recalculation is driven by an index rebuild or a plain re-read, and Stage 12 proves them directly — a confirmed holiday's shifted date is simply visible on `workingDays.*`'s very next call, with the same three record types correctly excluded. `VRS-F004`'s G06, G07, its Materialization section, its Non-Functional Requirements and its Decisions Recorded section are all corrected to record this staging split explicitly.

**Status: closed.** Stage 12 is unblocked; it builds the full feature except the index, which remains an explicit, tracked deferral rather than a silent gap. Whichever issue builds `VRS-F005` inherits the obligation to design and build G06/G07's index at that time.

### F227 — no defined working week for the founding Global Entity or two allowed jurisdictions

Raised 22 September 2026 tracing Stage 12 (`FDN-119`) before implementation. `VRS-F004` G02 and the Stage 12 Done criteria require an Active WorkingCalendar the moment *every* Entity is created. The two creation paths are Stage 11's `writeFoundingRecords` (a `Global` Entity in the workspace-creation transaction) and `entity.create`, whose closed jurisdiction enum also allows `IN` and `SG`. `VRS-F004`'s setup flow gives templates for PK, UK, US, AE and SA only. It gives no working week for `Global`, `IN` or `SG`; `VRS-F003` says `Global` uses the workspace's own default calendar, but neither the existing founding records nor the registered `Entity`→`WorkingCalendar` edge provides a separate workspace default. Neither creation API accepts the seven weekdays, and `VPS-F006`'s setup wizard, which would ask the founder, is not built. An implementation cannot create the required calendar without choosing a seven-day template and daily hours that the founder has not specified. Reusing a PK/UK template for `Global`, or silently using Monday–Friday for all three, would be a new product decision.

**Ruling — closed 22 September 2026.** `Global`, `IN` and `SG` all get the same initial template UK and US already use: Monday to Friday, `standard_daily_hours: 8` — the schema's own existing default for that field. This is not a new pattern; it is the same shape two of the five named templates already use, applied to the three the spec left unnamed, and it is editable afterward through `calendar.update` by an Owner or HR Admin, the same escape hatch F222 already established for the founding Entity's own placeholder defaults (`jurisdiction: "Global"`, `default_currency: "USD"`). Both creation paths write the calendar atomically with the Entity it belongs to, never as a follow-up: `entity.create`'s implementation and Stage 11's `writeFoundingRecords` (F222's founding bootstrap) both call one shared internal helper — `createInitialCalendar(tx, entityId, jurisdiction)` — that selects PK/UK/US/AE/SA's own named template when the jurisdiction matches, and the Monday-to-Friday/8-hour default otherwise, in the same transaction as the Entity write. `VRS-F004`'s setup-flow prose and its G02 are corrected to name this default explicitly rather than leaving `Global`/`IN`/`SG` unaddressed. AE's own named template is itself corrected from Monday-to-Friday to Sunday-to-Thursday the same day (F232), once tracing found it contradicted the feature's own Friday-exclusion acceptance criterion; the Global/IN/SG default above is unaffected by that fix. The jurisdiction-template selection this helper performs is also exported as a plain, portable function so the client's optimistic `entity.create` handler can select the identical template without a round trip (F233).

**Status: closed.** Stage 12 is unblocked on this point.

### F228 — Stage 12's lifecycle mutations omit the A003-T54 base version

`VPS-A003` A003-T54 requires every state-transition mutation to carry the caller's base version and reject `stale-state` if that version moved. F223 applied that exact rule to `entity.deactivate` in Stage 11, adding required `expected_version`. Stage 12's `calendar.update` marks the prior WorkingCalendar `Superseded`; `holiday.cancel` marks a Holiday `Canceled`; `pattern.set`/`pattern.clear` mark the prior WorkingPattern `Superseded`. `holiday.confirm` changes provisional confirmation state and its effective date. Their API signatures in `VRS-F004` and Do items 1–4 give no base-version argument. The shared mutation envelope contains no implicit base version. Reading the row's latest version inside `validate()` would not detect that the person's decision was made against an older value. Adding `expected_version` to these signatures, exempting any operation, or choosing a different conflict rule changes the stated contract.

**Ruling — closed 22 September 2026.** A003-T54's base-version requirement is read the same way F223 already established it for Entity: it applies to a mutation that changes a node's own `lifecycle_status` — a genuine state transition — not to every field write. By that rule: `calendar.update`, `holiday.cancel` and `pattern.set`/`pattern.clear` each move an existing node from `Active` to `Superseded`/`Canceled` and therefore require the caller's base version of *that node* (the one being closed, not the one being created); `holiday.add` creates a new node with nothing to version, exactly like `entity.create`; `holiday.confirm` changes `date`/`confirmed_at`/`confirmed_by` but never `lifecycle_status` — a Holiday stays `Active` whether provisional or confirmed — so it is an ordinary field update, exactly like `entity.update`, and carries no `expectedVersion`, matching that precedent rather than inventing a new one. Corrected signatures: `calendar.update(calendarId, workingWeek, dailyHours, expectedVersion)`, `holiday.cancel(holidayId, expectedVersion)`, `pattern.set(employeeId, workingWeek, effectiveFrom, expectedVersion?)` — optional, present only when a current pattern exists to close (omitted on an employee's first-ever pattern, exactly as there is nothing to version) — and `pattern.clear(employeeId, effectiveFrom, expectedVersion)`, always required since clearing always closes an existing pattern. Each `validate()` checks the given version against the row being closed first (`stale-state` on a mismatch), before any other business rule, exactly `entity.deactivate`'s own ordering. `VRS-F004`'s API contracts and the Stage 12 brief are corrected to carry these arguments.

**Status: closed.** Stage 12 is unblocked on this point.

### F229 — the pattern overlap refusal also rejects the replacement it requires

The Stage 12 brief says `pattern.set(employeeId, workingWeek, effectiveFrom)` closes the current Active WorkingPattern at `effectiveFrom`, marks it `Superseded`, and creates the next Active pattern. The same Do item says to reject if the new `effective_from` falls inside an existing pattern's still-open range. For a current pattern `[1 January, null)`, a normal change effective 1 March is inside the still-open range when validation runs. Taken literally, G04 rejects every second `pattern.set`, while permitting it requires treating the edge/node being atomically closed as an exception. The brief does not say whether a future-effective change is allowed, how backdating interacts with already-closed history, or whether a pending future pattern counts as current. Those choices decide whether history overlaps.

**Ruling — closed 22 September 2026.** The brief's stated overlap check was wrong as written: it treated the still-open range `pattern.set` is itself about to close as a rejection target, which rejects every legitimate replacement. `WorkingPattern`'s effective-dated boundary follows the same shared-boundary, half-open convention Stage 11 already established for `scoped_to_entity` (`closeAt`, edge closed *at* the new edge's own `effective_from`, no gap, no overlap): closing the current pattern sets its `effective_to` to the new pattern's `effective_from` itself, not the day before. G04's real overlap check is therefore not about the pattern being closed at all — it is a single guard: `pattern.set`'s `effectiveFrom` must be on or after the current Active pattern's own `effective_from`. A `effectiveFrom` earlier than that would fall inside an *already-Superseded* pattern's closed history (a genuine overlap G04 exists to prevent) rather than inside the one this call is closing, and is refused (`invalid-args`) rather than silently rewriting history. A same-day replacement (`effectiveFrom` equal to the current pattern's own `effective_from`) is allowed and simply supersedes it outright, zero-duration, exactly as a same-day `calendar.update` would. `pattern.clear` uses the identical guard and boundary convention, closing the current pattern with no replacement. `VRS-F004`'s G04 and Stage 12's Do item are corrected to state the check this way, reusing `closeAt`'s existing shape rather than inventing a second boundary convention.

**Status: closed.** Stage 12 is unblocked on this point.

### F230 — a superseding calendar has no defined relationship to existing holidays

`VRS-F004` says a WorkingCalendar defines an Entity's working week *and holiday set*, and G01 requires `calendar.update` to create a new WorkingCalendar node while the old one remains for history. Holiday has `calendar_id` and the registered `holiday_in` edge to one WorkingCalendar. After an update, every existing Holiday still points to the old calendar. Neither the spec nor Stage 12 says whether the new version copies those holidays, adds new edges to them, inherits them by traversing `supersedes`, or starts without them. The options produce different `workingDays.*` answers after an ordinary working-week edit and different historical results if a provisional holiday is later confirmed. Moving a Holiday to the new calendar may also erase the old version's holiday history; copying may duplicate the holiday and its confirmation state.

**Ruling — closed 22 September 2026.** `VRS-F004` already gives the reason a `WorkingCalendar` version exists at all: "so that a period computed under the previous working week stays correct" (G01). That reasoning applies identically to the holiday set a calendar defines — a period computed under the previous calendar's holidays must also stay correct, so holidays are versioned the same way the calendar itself is, rather than left ambiguous. `calendar.update` copies forward every currently-`Active` `Holiday` (never a `Canceled` one) belonging to the calendar version being superseded, writing a new `Holiday` node with identical fields (`name`, `date`, `holiday_type`, `applies_to_locations`, `is_provisional`, `estimated_date`, `confirmation_window_days`, `confirmed_at`, `confirmed_by`, `is_half_day`) and a new `holiday_in` edge to the new calendar version, in the same transaction as the calendar write itself. The old calendar version's own original `Holiday` nodes and edges are untouched — a query against the old, now-`Superseded` version (via `calendar.get(entityId, asOf)`) sees exactly the holiday set that was true at that time, forever. `holiday.confirm`/`holiday.cancel` operate on the *current Active* calendar's own holidays only — `validate()` refuses (`not-found`) a holiday belonging to a `Superseded` calendar version, since a historical holiday set is frozen the same way historical working weeks are. `VRS-F004`'s G01/G05 and `calendar.update`'s Do item are corrected to state this copy-forward explicitly.

**Status: closed.** Stage 12 is unblocked on this point.

### F231 — G07's non-deferred exceptions have no Stage 12 consumer or write path

F226 defers only the materialized index and its rebuild mechanism. Its detailed ruling explicitly says G07's three exceptions are *not* deferred and that Stage 12 proves them directly. `VRS-F004`'s acceptance criteria require a closed PayRun to remain unchanged with an adjustment raised in the next period; an approved LeaveRequest to keep its deduction while a notice reaches the approving manager; and a signed Contract to remain unchanged. The repository has registered node types and permission tests for these concepts but no PayRun, LeaveRequest or Contract feature writer, no next-period adjustment operation, and no manager-notice workflow for this event. Stage 12 Do item 5 only defines `workingDays.*` read functions, which will return the new confirmed hours on their next call. That proves the live-read half of G07, but neither creates an adjustment nor sends a notice, and a test that confirms no unrelated row changed would not prove the required effects. The brief gives no hook or deferred owner for them.

**Ruling — closed 22 September 2026, correcting F226's own overreach.** F226 said G07's three recalculation exemptions "are not deferred... Stage 12 proves them directly." That was wrong on inspection: F226 was right that a closed PayRun, an approved LeaveRequest and a signed Contract must each be *exempt* from recalculation, but proving an exemption requires the thing being exempted to exist, and none of `VRS-F062` (Payroll), leave's `LeaveRequest`, or `VRS-F020` (Contract) is built. Stage 12 has no PayRun, LeaveRequest or Contract writer, no next-period adjustment operation and no manager-notice workflow to test against — F231 is correct that a plain `workingDays.*` re-read cannot by itself raise either the adjustment or the notice, because nothing consumes `workingDays.*` at that granularity yet. Corrected split: Stage 12 proves only the general case G07's own text already separates from the three exemptions — "everything else... recalculates freely, because each is a live view rather than a committed record" — by confirming `workingDays.count`/`isWorking`/`hoursOn` return the newly-confirmed values on the very next call after a `holiday.confirm`, with nothing cached or stale. The three named exemptions (PayRun, LeaveRequest, Contract) are documented in `VRS-F004` as binding rules for whichever stage builds each consuming feature — each of those stages inherits the obligation to implement its own exemption and its own adjustment/notice behavior against a calendar shift, exactly as it inherits every other rule this document states about its own node type — but none of the three is Stage 12's to build or test. `VRS-F004`'s G07 and its acceptance criteria are corrected to mark the PayRun/LeaveRequest/Contract examples as forward-looking rules for their own future stages rather than Stage 12 acceptance criteria, and F226's own ruling paragraph and Stage 12's Done criteria are both corrected to match this narrower, accurate scope.

**Status: closed.** Stage 12 is unblocked; the resolution-order/write-model work (F227–F230) and the live-view half of G07 are all it needs to prove.

### F232 — AE's named template includes Friday while its acceptance criterion excludes Friday

Raised 22 September 2026 while resuming Stage 12 (`FDN-119`) on the F227–F231 rulings, before implementation. `VRS-F004`'s setup flow defines the named AE template as Monday through Friday, and F227 explicitly uses AE as one of the existing Monday-through-Friday templates. The same feature's first acceptance criterion says that, for an AE Entity with that Monday-to-Friday calendar, a Friday in the evaluated range is excluded as a non-working day. Stage 12's Done criteria require that exact “AE Friday exclusion” proof. ISO Friday is day 5, so one template cannot both mark it working and exclude it as non-working. The remaining likely templates are product choices: Sunday–Thursday, Monday–Thursday, or a different configured example rather than the initial template.

**Ruling — closed 22 September 2026.** The acceptance criterion is correct; the named-template line was the error. AE's real weekend has historically run Friday-Saturday (this document's own background section says so), the same reasoning that already gives Saudi Arabia its own Sunday-to-Thursday template. AE's named template is corrected to match: Sunday to Thursday, the same as SA. `createInitialCalendar`'s AE branch and the setup-flow prose (the line naming the five named templates) are both corrected; the Friday-exclusion acceptance criterion needs no change, since it was right all along. F227's own already-closed ruling is corrected in step: its "plurality template already used by UK/US/AE" rationale for the Global/IN/SG default is no longer accurate once AE moves — the Global/IN/SG Monday-to-Friday default itself is unaffected and stands on its own (the same default UK and US already use), only the supporting sentence is reworded.

**Status: closed.** Stage 12 is unblocked on this point.

### F233 — `entity.create` gains a server-side calendar write with no optimistic contract or IDs

Raised 22 September 2026 while tracing F227 through the existing Stage 11 mutation. F227 requires both `entity.create` and the founding bootstrap to call `createInitialCalendar` in the same server transaction, adding a WorkingCalendar node and its Entity ownership edge to `entity.create`'s authoritative effect. The existing optimistic `entity.create` in `packages/graph/src/mutators/foundation.ts` creates only the Entity node. Stage 12 Do item 7 names optimistic handlers only for the six newly registered mutations and does not amend `entity.create`. This would leave an offline creation without the Active calendar G02 says exists “from the moment it is created,” and the client would optimistically apply only part of the mutation the server later commits.

The missing part cannot be filled mechanically. The Entity ID is derived portably from `mutationId` today, but there is no separate deterministic derivation for the initial calendar or its ownership edge, and the required helper is described as a server transaction helper. Choosing random server IDs makes the optimistic rows disagree with replication; inventing new deterministic derivations and duplicating template selection on the client changes the shared mutation contract.

**Ruling — closed 22 September 2026.** Yes: `entity.create`'s optimistic handler creates the calendar node and its `Entity`→`WorkingCalendar` ownership edge in the same call, exactly mirroring the server's atomic write (F227) — an offline-created Entity must have its calendar the moment it is created, per G02, with no gap where only the Entity exists locally. Two new functions join `moveEmployeeEdgeId` in `packages/schema/src/mutations/foundation.ts`, reusing its exact shape (a one-to-one bit-flip of the `mutationId`, a distinct constant each, so all three IDs derived from one mutation never collide with each other or with any other mutation's derived IDs): one derives the calendar's `node_id`, one derives the ownership edge's `edge_id`. `createInitialCalendar`'s jurisdiction-template table (F227, AE corrected by F232) is extracted into one small, portable, pure function in `packages/schema` — no I/O, no transaction — that both the server helper and the client's `entityCreate` optimistic mutator call, so the two can never select different templates for the same jurisdiction. Stage 11's server-only founding bootstrap has no client-cache counterpart and is unaffected.

**Status: closed.** Stage 12 is unblocked on this point.

### F234 — reduced-hours periods are required for reads but have no named write path

Raised 22 September 2026 while tracing Stage 12's required UAE Ramadan acceptance test into the corrected mutation surface. `VRS-F004` defines `reduced_hours_periods` as an inline JSON array on WorkingCalendar, makes its factor the final step of the working-day resolution order, describes add/edit/remove UI, and requires a UAE Ramadan period at factor `0.75` to return six hours. It also says provisional periods have a confirmation mechanism. Yet the only WorkingCalendar write is the exact corrected signature `calendar.update(calendarId, workingWeek, dailyHours, expectedVersion)`, which accepts no reduced-hours periods. No other named mutation creates, edits, removes or confirms an inline period. Initial jurisdiction templates specify only weekdays and standard daily hours.

Under `VPS-A003`, the graph can be written only through a named mutation. An integration test could bypass that rule by inserting a crafted node directly, but the product could never create the state the test pretends to exercise, and the required optimistic handler would still have no contract to mirror.

**Ruling — closed 22 September 2026.** `reduced_hours_periods` is edited exclusively through `calendar.update`, never through a mutation of its own — exactly consistent with its own schema note that it is held "on WorkingCalendar rather than as its own node, since it has no independent lifecycle," and with F225's own already-recorded phrase that its confirmation record is "the superseding WorkingCalendar version's own stamp," which only makes sense if confirming one supersedes the calendar. `calendar.update` gains one more optional parameter: `reducedHoursPeriods?`. Omitted, the new version carries the prior version's array forward unchanged — the same copy-forward convention F230 gave Holiday. Provided, it replaces the array wholesale on the new version: adding a period, editing one, removing one and confirming a provisional one (rewriting its `is_provisional`/dates) are all simply a new array passed to the same call, with no separate write path, no new node type and no dedicated confirmation mutation. The optimistic `calendar.update` handler (F233's sibling work, same Do item) carries `reducedHoursPeriods` through the identical copy-forward-or-replace logic.

**Status: closed.** Stage 12 is unblocked; F227 through F234 together give it everything G01–G08 need to be built and tested.

### F235 — `pattern_for` has no governing Employee partition, so no pattern can be written

Raised 23 September 2026 during Stage 12 implementation (`FDN-119`) when the first real `pattern.set` integration test reached the server interceptor. The mutation successfully constructs its WorkingPattern node and required `pattern_for` edge check, but `authorizeWrite` refuses the edge with `reason: "role"` before apply. This is deterministic under the existing F136 rule, rather than a missing role-matrix row: `pattern_for` connects `WorkingPattern` to `Employee`; Employee has two protection partitions (`operational` and `compensation`); and `packages/schema/src/registry/edges.ts` declares no `governingPartitions` entry for this relationship. `edgeRoleDecision` therefore refuses it before considering whether any caller has Full access. Owner, HR Admin, and every other role are equally unable to create the edge.

The Stage 12 brief says the existing WorkingCalendar, Holiday and WorkingPattern edge registrations are complete and need no changes. They are structurally registered, but `pattern_for` is not writable through the server-authoritative interceptor. This is the same class of omission F208 found for `managed_by` and `scoped_to_entity`; that finding required an explicit founder ruling before both relationships were assigned Employee's `operational` partition and the declaration was recorded in `VPS-A002`. WorkingPattern is Tier 0 scheduling data and `VRS-F004` describes it as operationally necessary, so `operational` is the direct precedent, but assigning the reviewed partition is an authorization decision and must not be inferred silently by implementation.

**Ruling — closed 23 September 2026.** `governingPartitions: { Employee: "operational" }` is declared on `pattern_for` in `packages/schema/src/registry/edges.ts`, exactly F208's precedent for `managed_by` and `scoped_to_entity`: which working pattern applies to an employee — like who they report to, or which entity employs them — is operational HR/scheduling data, never a compensation fact. `WorkingPattern` itself carries no split of its own — it is registered `fixedProtection("Standard", 0)`, a single-partition Tier 0 node — so only the `Employee` endpoint needed a declaration. Recorded in `VPS-A002`'s `governingPartitions` table with a one-line rationale, alongside `has_skill`, `holds_certification`, `assignment_of`, `managed_by` and `scoped_to_entity`. The Stage 12 brief's claim that the four working-calendar edges were "already complete... none of that needs touching" is corrected: the endpoint pairs are structurally registered, but `pattern_for`'s write authorization needed this one reviewed declaration before any role could ever create the edge — a gap in how thoroughly the brief traced F136's rule against a split endpoint, not a gap in the registration itself.

**Status: closed.** Stage 12 is unblocked; the partial implementation preserved on `codex/stage-12-working-calendar` (`7e64708`) resumes on this ruling.

### F236 — VRS-F005's "entirely local" claims include Tier 1/2 data that F199's architecture never caches on device

Raised 23 September 2026 while briefing Stage 13 (`VRS-F005`), reading the spec directly before writing the brief.

**The contradiction.** The spec states three times that the Contextual Intelligence Panel is "entirely local" (the Selecting a row flow, the Panel's own section header, and G04's "resolved entirely from the local store"), immediately followed in each case by a description of the exact same traversal surfacing BurnoutAlert and FlightRiskSignal — both registered `fixedProtection("Manager-restricted"/"Owner and HR Admin only", 2)`, Tier 2. `benchForecast.get`'s API contract claims to resolve "entirely from the local index. No network round-trip," while the same section's Bench computation defines accumulated cost as a function of the employee's compensation, and `Employee` is registered split-protection with its compensation half at Tier 1 (`Finance-restricted`). The feature's Non-Functional Requirements and its offline acceptance criterion both claim full offline functionality "with no read degradation." Per F199's rewritten `VPS-A003`, Tier 0 is the only tier Electric replicates into the device cache; **Tier 1 and Tier 2 are "field-encrypted... fetched on demand... held in memory only"** — never stored on a device, online or off. None of the spec's "entirely local" language is true for BurnoutAlert, FlightRiskSignal, or the bench cost currency figure; all three require a live `protected.read` round-trip under the architecture this feature is about to be built on.

**What is not affected.** The 90-day timeline's bars, dates and bench-day counts genuinely are Tier 0 — `Assignment`, `Project`, `Client`, `GhostResource`, `OpenRole` and `effective_billing_rate` are all registered `fixedProtection("Standard", 0)` — so the timeline's own "no network request" acceptance criterion is correct as written and needs no change. The Contextual Intelligence Panel's `skills`/`openRoles`/`references` fields are Tier 0 too and stay genuinely local.

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 23 September 2026.** The spec is corrected, not the architecture — the same class of correction F199 itself made to `VRS-F002`/`VPS-F002`, and exactly the "just-in-time, before its own build stage" pattern FDN-104 already established.
1. The Contextual Intelligence Panel resolves its Tier 0 fields (skills, open roles, references) from the device's local Electric-replicated cache, as before. Its Tier 2 fields (BurnoutAlert, FlightRiskSignal) are fetched on demand from the server the instant the Panel opens, gated by `VPS-A004`'s interceptor exactly as `contextualIntelligence.get`'s contract already scopes them, and held in memory only per F199 — never cached. This is one additional network call per Panel open, not zero.
2. Bench cost is split from the rest of `benchForecast.get`. The timeline's bars, dates and bench-day counts stay genuinely local and unchanged. The currency-denominated accumulated cost figure is compensation-derived (Tier 1) and cannot resolve from the device cache; it is fetched by a new, separate, batched on-demand call for the visible cohort — `benchForecast.getCost(employeeIds, window)` — issued after the local render completes and filled in asynchronously, never blocking the 200ms budget. An unauthorized viewer still receives no currency figure, unchanged from the spec's existing rule.
3. The WellnessTriggerEvent exclusion is reworded: not because "an unauthorized device never holds the data to suppress" (a device-sync framing this architecture no longer has), but because `contextualIntelligence.get` never requests that node type at all — Tier 3 stays a deferred end-to-end module per F199, with no read path in this feature regardless of role.
4. The offline claims are scoped to what F199 actually guarantees offline: the timeline, bars, bench regions and day counts (all Tier 0) render fully offline, unchanged. Bench cost figures and the Panel's Tier 2 content require connectivity and show `VPS-D004`'s standard connectivity-required state while offline, exactly as every other Tier 1/2 read in the product now does.

`VRS-F005`'s Depends On line, its Selecting-a-row flow text, its Contextual Intelligence Panel section, its API contracts, G04, its offline acceptance criterion and its "fully functional offline" NFR are all corrected to this reading.

**Status: closed.** Stage 13 is unblocked on this point.

### F237 — the deferred working-day index, inherited from F226/F231, is resolved without building one

Raised 23 September 2026 while briefing Stage 13 (`VRS-F005`), the feature F226 and F231 both name as the eventual owner of the deferred materialized working-day index.

**The inheritance.** F226 (closed during Stage 12's briefing) deferred `VRS-F004`'s G06 (the index's existence and materialization) and the index-rebuild half of G07 "in full to whichever stage builds `VRS-F005`," on the reasoning that the index's only reason to exist is this feature's own 200ms/150-employee render budget, and that it should be "designed against the server-authoritative architecture as it stands at that time, not against the retired one this document's prose still describes." `VRS-F004`'s own Materialization section describes the original design: "a working-day index... materialized in the SQLite-WASM index... consuming features read this index; they never walk the graph," rebuilt incrementally on five trigger events. That description predates the September 2026 pivot and was written against Loro's client-side CRDT materialization pipeline, not the current one.

**What actually changed underneath it.** Under F199/F201, `wa-sqlite` is retained but is now "fed by replication rather than by CRDT materialization" — it is an ordinary local SQL index over Electric-replicated Tier 0 rows, not a bespoke Worker-private materialization step. `WorkingCalendar`, `Holiday` and `WorkingPattern` are all Tier 0 (confirmed directly against `packages/schema/src/registry/nodes.ts`), so every row the resolution order in `VRS-F004`'s "Resolution order" section needs is already present in the device's local cache today, with no additional replication design required. Stage 12 built `workingDays.*` server-side, unindexed, evaluating the resolution order directly per call — correct for Stage 12's own callers, but server-only, which does not by itself give the Bench Forecast a local, no-network evaluation path; calling that server API per render would break `benchForecast.get`'s "no network round-trip" claim (see F236, which resolves the parts of that claim actually invalidated by Tier 1/2 data — this finding is about the Tier 0 timeline computation itself, which the "no network" claim correctly describes provided the resolution logic is available locally).

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 23 September 2026.** No separate materialized index table is built. The resolution-order logic — the same four-step Holiday → WorkingPattern → WorkingCalendar → ReducedHoursPeriod evaluation `workingDays.*` already implements server-side — is extracted into a portable, pure, no-I/O function in `packages/schema` (the same pattern F233 already established for `initialWorkingWeekFor`: one canonical implementation, called from both sides, never duplicated). `services/api`'s existing `workingDays.*` calls it unchanged; the Bench Forecast's client-side renderer calls the identical function directly against the device's local Tier 0 cache (`WorkingCalendar`, `Holiday`, `WorkingPattern`, already replicated), never through a server round-trip. At 150 employees × 90 cells (13,500 evaluations of a plain function over already-local SQL rows, not a graph traversal through a sync protocol), this comfortably meets the 200ms render and 50ms filter budgets without precomputation — the same "no cache, no precomputed table, prove it against the real numbers before building one" judgment F226 itself already applied to Stage 12, now applied to the stage F226 pointed at. If Stage 13's own performance testing shows the unindexed evaluation does not meet budget at real data volumes, that is a new finding to raise then, grounded in a real measurement — not a reason to build the original index speculatively now. `VRS-F004`'s G06/G07 and Materialization section, and `VRS-F005`'s Technical Architecture, are both corrected to record this resolution in place of the open deferral.

**Status: closed.** Stage 13 is unblocked; it owns no separate index-building obligation.

### F238 — the k-anonymity disclosure-control mechanism VRS-F005 depends on has never been built

Raised 23 September 2026 while briefing Stage 13 (`VRS-F005`), checking `VPS-A004`'s "Aggregate disclosure control" section against the actual codebase before writing the brief.

**The gap.** `VRS-F005`'s own Security Considerations require "aggregate utilization passes through `VPS-A004`'s disclosure control, including the differencing protection," and four of its ten acceptance criteria depend on a below-threshold cohort being suppressed. `VPS-A004` specifies the mechanism in full: a cohort below `k_anonymity_minimum` (default 5) or `k_anonymity_minimum_sensitive` (default 8, Tier 3-derived aggregates) is suppressed rather than approximated; a filter reducing a displayed cohort by fewer than `k` members returns the unfiltered aggregate instead of a new, smaller-cohort one; and a sequence of aggregate queries against overlapping cohorts is recorded in `VPS-F004`'s Silent Audit Log. A repository-wide search (`k_anonymity`, `kAnonymity`, `Suppressed`) found no implementation anywhere — the mechanism has never been built, by any stage. The two threshold values are documented as workspace settings owned by `VPS-F005_Workspace_Configuration_Console` (Privacy and data section, with a hard floor of 3), and that feature is not built either — there is no configuration surface, and no Workspace field currently holds either value.

**Why this cannot be deferred like a partial forward dependency.** `VRS-F006`/`VRS-F007` (Rate Card, Ghost Resources) are explicitly non-blocking per the spec's own "Partial forward dependencies" line — assignment cost and Ghost rows simply render degraded until those features land. Aggregate suppression is different: it is this feature's own acceptance criteria, not a forward-looking allowance, and a founder who filters the Bench Forecast to a three-person cohort and sees a real percentage instead of the required restricted state is exactly the disclosure failure `VPS-A004` exists to prevent.

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 23 September 2026.**
1. **Settings, not UI.** `k_anonymity_minimum` (default 5) and `k_anonymity_minimum_sensitive` (default 8) are added directly to `Workspace`'s own schema now, with the documented floor of 3 enforced wherever they are read. No settings screen is built — `VPS-F005_Workspace_Configuration_Console` still owns editing them later, exactly the "data field now, dedicated editing surface later" split F222 already used for the founding Entity's placeholder defaults and F227 used for the Global/IN/SG calendar template.
2. **One shared mechanism, not a bespoke check.** A single function — e.g. `applyDisclosureControl(cohort, aggregateFn, threshold)` in `packages/schema`'s policy module, alongside `MATRIX_OVERRIDES` — implements exactly `VPS-A004`'s two enforceable rules: suppress below `k`; on a filter that would reduce an already-displayed cohort by fewer than `k` members, return the unfiltered aggregate with an indication the filter wasn't applied to the figure, rather than compute a new one. `VRS-F005`'s `benchForecast.get` is its first caller, built generally enough (cohort in, aggregate function in, threshold in) that a later feature (`VRS-F048`, `VRS-F058`, `VRS-F059`, `VRS-F060`, `VRS-F071`, `VRS-F072`, `VRS-F011`) calls the same function rather than reimplementing it, per `VPS-A004`'s own explicit intent that "every aggregate in this product passes through one mechanism."
3. **The differencing-attempt audit trail is deferred, recorded rather than silently dropped.** `VPS-F004`'s `AuditEntry.event_type` is a closed, access/denial-focused enum (F206, F221, F225's precedent) with no category for an ordinary aggregate read, successful or suppressed. Extending it to log every aggregate query for pattern-of-differencing detection is a `VPS-F004` schema change with its own tradeoffs (query volume, retention), not a one-line addition inside this feature. Stage 13 builds and tests rules 1 and 2 above; the audit trail is a named follow-up, owned by whichever stage next extends `VPS-F004`'s event taxonomy or builds a dedicated aggregate-access log.

`VPS-A002`'s `Workspace` node schema and `VRS-F005`'s Security Considerations are corrected to record the two new fields and point at the shared mechanism; `VPS-F005_Workspace_Configuration_Console` is unaffected — it still owns the settings screen when it is eventually built.

**Status: closed.** Stage 13 is unblocked; it builds the mechanism for real rather than special-casing it, and records the deferred audit trail rather than skipping it silently.

### F239 — the Stage 13 local Bench Forecast query cannot implement its required Pitch-day exclusion

Raised 23 September 2026 while tracing Stage 13 (`VRS-F005`) from its corrected brief into the device-cache query layer, before implementation.

**The conflict.** `VRS-F005` defines bench time as working days with no Active Assignment coverage **less every day carrying Pitch-categorized time**. G02 repeats that rule, and an acceptance criterion requires six Pitch days to disappear from the bench region and its cost. The same spec says the Pitch exclusion moved into this feature so it would be implemented once rather than supplied by `VRS-F012` later.

Stage 13 Do item 3 defines the local query over `Assignment`, `Project`, `Client`, `GhostResource`, `OpenRole`, `Entity`, `WorkingCalendar`, `Holiday` and `WorkingPattern`. It does not include `TimesheetEntry`, the only record that can say a person logged Pitch time. Checked directly against the registry: `TimesheetEntry` (owner `VRS-F010`), `Pitch` (owner `VRS-F009`) and the `logged_against` edge connecting them (owner `VRS-F010`) are all structurally registered, but `packages/schema` has no field schema beyond the registry entry, no named mutation, and no typed local query contract for any of the three — a repository-wide search for a mutation touching either node type found none. `VRS-001`'s Feature Register confirms `VRS-F009` (F18) and `VRS-F010` (F08) both sit strictly after `VRS-F005` (F03) in MVP order.

**Why this is the same class of gap as F236, not a new one.** `VRS-F005`'s own Depends-On section already distinguishes hard dependencies from partial forward dependencies, and already names exactly this pattern twice: `VRS-F006` supplies rate resolution ("until it exists, `effective_billing_rate` is written as `billing_rate_default`") and `VRS-F007` supplies Ghost rows ("until it exists, none are rendered"). Both are explicit, both degrade gracefully, and neither blocks this feature. `VRS-F009`/`VRS-F010`'s Pitch-time dependency is structurally identical — G02's own prose simply never received the same treatment, so it reads as an unconditional requirement Stage 13 cannot meet.

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 23 September 2026.** `VRS-F005`'s Partial forward dependencies line is extended to name `VRS-F009` and `VRS-F010`: until both exist, the Pitch exclusion is not applied, and bench periods compute from Assignment coverage alone. This degrades toward false amber — a person pursuing new business reads as bench-idle until the taxonomy exists to say otherwise — and never toward hiding a real gap or fabricating data, the same safe-direction reasoning F226 applied to deferring the working-day index and F236 applied to deferring Tier 2 Panel content. G02's table row, the bench computation formula's prose, the Pitch acceptance criterion, the Out of Scope list and the Decisions Recorded note are all corrected to record the deferral rather than describe unconditional behavior Stage 13 cannot build. Stage 13's Do item 3 query contract needs no correction — it was already right to omit `TimesheetEntry`; the spec was wrong to imply otherwise.

**Status: closed.** Stage 13 is unblocked; it builds no `TimesheetEntry`, `Pitch` or `logged_against` write or read path, and the Pitch-day exclusion becomes a named follow-up for whichever stage builds `VRS-F009`/`VRS-F010`.

### F240 — Assignment cancellation omits the mandatory state-transition base version

Raised 23 September 2026 while resuming Stage 13 (`FDN-120`) on F239's ruling, before any product code was written.

**The conflict.** Stage 13 Do item 1 and `VRS-F005`'s API contract both specify `assignment.cancel(assignmentId) -> { success }`. Cancellation changes the Assignment's state from `Active` to `Canceled`; `VRS-F005` names those values in Assignment's schema, and the registry uses the fixed lifecycle policy for this node type. `VPS-A003` A003-T54 requires every state-transition mutation to carry the base `version` the decision was made against and requires the server to reject `stale-state` when that version has changed. The shared mutation envelope carries no implicit base version.

This is the exact contract gap F223 resolved for `entity.deactivate` and F228 resolved for `holiday.cancel`, `calendar.update`, `pattern.set` and `pattern.clear`.

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 23 September 2026, on F223/F228's own reading of A003-T54.** The contract gains a required `expectedVersion`: `assignment.cancel(assignmentId, expectedVersion) -> { success }`. `validate()` checks it first — `stale-state` before anything else — then transitions `status` from `Active` to `Canceled`, the same version-first ordering F223 established for `entity.deactivate` and F228 established for `holiday.cancel`/`calendar.update`/`pattern.set`/`pattern.clear`. `assignment.create` and `assignment.update` need no version argument: `create` has none to check against, and `update` never touches `status` — the same ordinary-field-write exemption F228 gave `holiday.confirm`. The optimistic handler mirrors `holidayCancel`'s exact shape in `packages/graph/src/mutators/foundation.ts`: compare the cached Assignment's version to `expectedVersion`, throw `OptimisticRejection("stale-state")` on mismatch, otherwise write `status: "Canceled"` locally against the checked version. `VRS-F005`'s API contract and Stage 13's Do items 1 and 7 are both corrected to the new signature and behavior.

**Status: closed.** Stage 13 is unblocked.


### F241 — Bench aggregate locality contradicts VPS-A004's single-enforcement-point rule for disclosure control

Raised 23 September 2026 while resuming Stage 13 (`FDN-120`) on F240's ruling, before any product code was written.

**The conflict.** `VRS-F005`'s `benchForecast.get` contract bundles `aggregateUtilization` and `cohortSize` into the same call as `rows`, with a comment claiming the whole call "resolves entirely from the device's local Electric-replicated cache. No network round-trip." Its offline acceptance criterion and the "Filter application completes within 50ms from the local graph" NFR repeat the same local claim for the filtered aggregate specifically. Stage 13 Do item 4 already computes aggregate utilization server-side and passes it through the shared `applyDisclosureControl` function (item 5) — but returns it "from `benchForecast.get`'s aggregate portion," naming no separate endpoint, so the two reviewer-authoritative documents describe the same return value as both local and server-computed.

**Why the contradiction resolves toward the server, not the local cache.** `VPS-A004`'s own "Where enforcement runs" section states the rule in terms that leave no reading: "The interceptor runs in `services/api`, and nowhere else decides access... A stream written by hand, or a client-side visibility rule, is a second answer to 'who may read this' and is prohibited." Its "Aggregate disclosure control" section names the Bench Forecast's filtered utilization by name as the reason the mechanism exists at all — "the Bench Forecast's filtered utilization percentage, which can identify an individual in a small workspace as readily as any survey result" — and one of its three differencing-protection requirements is that "sequences of aggregate queries against overlapping cohorts are recorded in `VPS-F004`'s Silent Audit Log," a server-side write no client function can perform. F238's own ruling built `applyDisclosureControl` as a generic, portable function, but portability of the function's code is not the same claim as where it is permitted to run — `VPS-A004` restricts that separately, and F238 never addressed the restriction. Stage 13 Do item 4 read `VPS-A004` correctly; `VRS-F005`'s locality claim for the aggregate, and the 50ms budget bound to "the local graph," did not.

This is the same class of correction F236 already made once in this spec: `benchForecast.get`'s bench-cost figure was pulled out into `benchForecast.getCost` because compensation data is Tier 1 and cannot resolve locally. Aggregate utilization needs the identical treatment for a Tier-0-adjacent but access-control-gated reason — the underlying `billable_percentage` rows are Tier 0 and already on-device, but the aggregate built from them is a disclosure surface `VPS-A004` requires the interceptor to gate, which a device cannot do to itself.

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 23 September 2026.**
1. **A new named endpoint, mirroring `getCost`'s existing split.** `benchForecast.getAggregate(workspaceId, window, filters?) -> { aggregateUtilization: number | Suppressed, cohortSize: number }` is added to `VRS-F005`'s API contracts, gated by the same permission interceptor `VPS-A004` requires and calling `applyDisclosureControl` server-side. It is a single on-demand call issued after item 3's local render (bars, dates, bench-day counts), reissued whenever the filter changes, and never blocks the local render — the same non-blocking shape F236 already established for `benchForecast.getCost`.
2. **`benchForecast.get` narrows to exactly what is genuinely local.** Its return type drops `aggregateUtilization` and `cohortSize`; its comment is corrected to describe only rows/bars/dates/bench-day counts as local, matching what Stage 13 Do item 3 already (correctly) built without needing correction itself.
3. **The 50ms budget is split, not removed.** Local filter matching (which employees match skill/seniority/availability) stays bound to the existing "completes within 50ms" / "from the local graph" language, since matching rows against filter criteria is genuinely local. Aggregate recomputation for the filtered cohort is reassigned its own non-blocking, post-render budget — the acceptance criterion and NFR are corrected to name the two as separate operations rather than one local claim.
4. **Offline behavior gains one more entry.** The offline acceptance criterion and the "Fully functional offline for Tier 0 data" NFR bullet add aggregate utilization and cohort size to the list of features that require connectivity, alongside bench cost and the Contextual Intelligence Panel's Tier 2 content — the timeline's bars, bench regions and day counts remain the only guarantee.

`VRS-F005`'s API contracts, G06, the filter/offline acceptance criteria, the NFR list and Security Considerations are all corrected to name `benchForecast.getAggregate` and its server-side enforcement explicitly. Stage 13 Do item 4 needed no change to its computation — only to name the endpoint instead of attributing the result to `benchForecast.get`; item 5's `applyDisclosureControl` description is corrected to name `benchForecast.getAggregate` as its first caller instead.

**Status: closed.** Stage 13 is unblocked; the aggregate boundary now has one server-side call, one named contract, and one local contract, with nothing attributed to both.


### F242 — The Bench Forecast's `filters` argument has no defined schema or matching semantics

Raised 23 September 2026 while resuming Stage 13 (`FDN-120`) on F241's ruling, before any product code was written.

**The gap.** F241 established `benchForecast.getAggregate(workspaceId, window, filters?)` as the authoritative server call for filtered aggregate utilization, alongside the same `filters?` parameter already present on the local `benchForecast.get`. `VRS-F005`'s Filtering section names five user-facing filter dimensions — skill, seniority, department, entity, availability window — in prose, but neither it nor the API contracts define field names, value types, empty-filter behavior, how multiple values within one dimension combine, how the five dimensions combine with each other, or what an "availability window" match actually means: at least one bench day in the window, wholly unassigned throughout it, available on its first day, or available by its end date all produce different cohorts.

**Why this cannot be worked around locally.** Passing the local query's matched employee IDs to `benchForecast.getAggregate` is not an available fix: F241 already ruled that the client cannot be a second authority for who is visible, precisely because a device choosing its own disclosure cohort is the same "client-side visibility rule" `VPS-A004` prohibits. The server must derive both the filtered cohort and the unfiltered comparison cohort itself, from the caller's own authorized base cohort — which means the server needs the same filter definition the client used to decide what the person was looking at, not a list of IDs standing in for it. Without one shared, precise contract, the local row query and the server aggregate can silently disagree on membership, and the disclosure-control comparison in F238's `applyDisclosureControl` (filtered cohort size vs. unfiltered cohort size) becomes meaningless if the two sides do not agree what "filtered" means.

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 23 September 2026.**
1. **One filter type, five fields, AND across fields, OR within a field.** `BenchForecastFilters = { skillIds?, seniorityLevels?, departments?, entityIds?, availability?: { fromDate, toDate } }`. An employee matches the whole object only if they satisfy every field that was specified; within a field, multiple values match on any one of them (a two-skill selection means either skill). An omitted `filters` argument, or one with every field omitted, applies no narrowing — it returns the caller's full authorized cohort, the same result as viewing the forecast with nothing selected.
2. **Each field's match is grounded in an existing schema fact, not a new one.** `skillIds` matches the `has_skill` edge; `seniorityLevels` matches `Employee.seniority_level`'s already-closed enum; `departments` matches `Employee.department` by exact string equality, since the Filters row is a Select populated from the workspace's own existing values, not typed text, so no normalization question exists; `entityIds` matches the employee's currently active `scoped_to_entity` edge (this feature computes everything live at render time, per G01–G07, so a historical at-any-point-in-window reading is out of scope); `availability` matches an employee with at least one G02 bench day inside `[fromDate, toDate]` inclusive — reusing the same derivation Stage 13 Do item 3 already computes, rather than inventing a second, competing definition of "available."
3. **One shared function, called identically by both sides.** `matchesBenchForecastFilters(candidate, filters)` — pure, no I/O, taking an already-derived per-employee shape (skill IDs, seniority, department, current entity, bench-day set) and the filter object, returning a boolean — is added to `packages/schema` alongside `resolveWorkingDay` (F237) and `applyDisclosureControl` (F238). Stage 13's local row query (item 3) and `benchForecast.getAggregate` (item 4) both call it, so the rows a person sees and the cohort size behind the aggregate they see can never diverge on filter logic.
4. **The server derives both cohorts itself.** `benchForecast.getAggregate` computes the filtered cohort and the unfiltered comparison cohort directly from the caller's own authorized base cohort (a Manager's direct reports, or workspace-wide for a role with broader read, exactly as the forecast itself is already scoped) using `matchesBenchForecastFilters` against its own server-side data — never from a client-supplied employee-ID list, which is the workaround F241 already ruled out.

`VRS-F005`'s Filtering section, API contracts, a new G08 and Security Considerations are corrected to carry this contract explicitly; Stage 13 Do items 3 and 4 are corrected to name `matchesBenchForecastFilters` as their shared filter-matching function.

**Status: closed.** Stage 13 is unblocked; the local query and the server aggregate share one filter definition, so their cohorts cannot silently disagree.


### F243 — Assignment's `governed_by` edge to RateCard is registered but never written by any contract

Raised 23 September 2026 while checking the code directly before writing the Stage 14 brief (`VRS-F006`), before any product code was written.

**The gap.** `VPS-A002`'s edge registry lists `governed_by` four times against four unrelated pairs, one of them `Assignment → RateCard`, owned by `VRS-F006` — confirmed directly against `packages/schema/src/registry/edges.ts`, where the pairing is real, running registry code, not merely a documentation table. But `VRS-F006`'s own Technical Architecture and API contracts describe rate resolution entirely in terms of Assignment's scalar `rate_card_id` field — already built by Stage 13, matching `VRS-F005`'s Assignment schema (`rate_card_id: UUID, nullable, FK to RateCard`) — and neither `assignment.setRateCard` nor `assignment.create` is ever described as writing the registered edge. Left as specified, the registry declares a relationship no mutation would ever create: `rateCard.usageCount(rateCardId) -> { activeAssignments }` and the rate card editor's version-history "how many assignments still point at it" figure would have no traversal path to answer from, only a full scan of every Assignment row — which A002-T03 already forbids outright: "All edges MUST be first-class objects with their own UUID. Implicit foreign key joins not materialized as edge records are prohibited." FDN-75 closed the identical class of gap once before, for `assigned_to` (Employee → Project "Via Assignment") registered with no materialized edge to the intermediate node.

**Why this isn't a contradiction to resolve by dropping the edge.** Assignment already has a precedent for exactly this shape: `employee_id` and `project_id` are both scalar fields on the Assignment record (per `VRS-F005`'s own schema block) *and* Assignment separately carries `assignment_of` and `assigned_to` edges to the same two nodes, written alongside the scalar fields by Stage 13's mutators — the scalar field serves Assignment's own fast reads, and the edge exists so the *other* side (an Employee or Project) can traverse back to find its Assignments. `rate_card_id` needs the identical shape for the identical reason: Assignment's own resolution algorithm reads the scalar field directly (no cross-feature traversal needed to price itself), while RateCard-side queries (`usageCount`, version history) need to traverse the other direction, which only a materialized edge supports, and which `VPS-A002`'s registry already anticipated by attributing `governed_by`'s ownership to this feature specifically.

**Why the edge is managed manually, not through `historyPolicy`.** `VPS-A002` names only `managed_by` and `scoped_to_entity` as registered for the single-active-outgoing-edge-with-history pattern; `governed_by` carries no such declaration, and adding one now would silently change behavior for the other three unbuilt `governed_by` pairs (`VRS-F018`, `VRS-F062`, `VRS-F063`) this stage has no mandate to design for. The established precedent for a singular governing relationship without that declaration is `governed_by_calendar` (Entity → WorkingCalendar, built in Stage 12): `services/api/src/mutations/calendar.ts` manages singularity itself, closing the entity's prior `governed_by_calendar` edge before opening the new one, rather than relying on an automatic registry mechanism. `governed_by`/Assignment→RateCard follows the same shape.

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 23 September 2026.**
1. **The scalar field is unchanged.** Assignment's existing `rate_card_id` (built by Stage 13, unmodified by this ruling) remains the value the rate-resolution algorithm itself reads — no cross-feature edge traversal is needed for an Assignment to resolve its own rate.
2. **`assignment.setRateCard` and `assignment.create` (when `rate_card_id` is provided) additionally write the registered `governed_by` edge**, from the Assignment to the chosen RateCard, mirroring the dual scalar-plus-edge shape `employee_id`/`assignment_of` and `project_id`/`assigned_to` already establish.
3. **Singularity is managed manually, mirroring `governed_by_calendar`.** Before writing a new `governed_by` edge from an Assignment, any existing active one from that Assignment is closed (`effective_to` set to the write's timestamp); the new edge opens with `effective_from` at the same instant. No `historyPolicy` declaration is added to the shared `governed_by` edge type.
4. **`rateCard.usageCount` and the version-history "how many assignments still point at it" figure both resolve via `incoming(tx, workspaceId, rateCardId, "governed_by")`**, counting only edges with `effective_to: null` for the live count, never a scan of Assignment rows.

`VRS-F006`'s Technical Architecture, API contracts and Graph Specifications are corrected to state this explicitly, as a new G07.

**Status: closed.** Stage 14 is unblocked; the registered edge has a contract, `rateCard.usageCount` has a traversal path, and Assignment's own resolution needs no change to already-merged Stage 13 code.


### F244 — `VRS-F006`'s NFRs and System States describe Tier 1 data as syncing to and aging out on devices

Raised 23 September 2026 while checking the code directly before writing the Stage 14 brief, before any product code was written.

**The gap.** `VRS-F006`'s Non-Functional Requirements state "RateCard and RateCardLine sync only to Owner, Finance Admin and HR Admin devices per `VPS-A003`," its System States table gives "Aged out" the treatment "Superseded cards outside `VPS-A003`'s Tier 1 window render dashed with **Fetch**," and its Depends-On line cites `VPS-A003` for "Tier 1 sync and encryption." `VPS-A003` itself says the opposite of all three: Tier 1's device state is "Memory only, fetched on demand; never persisted to device storage," and a later section states plainly that "Tier 1 recovery holders, the threshold scheme and the retention window are withdrawn... `tier1_retention_window_months` is deprecated." There is no sync, no per-role device cache, and no window for anything to age out of — every Tier 1 read is a fresh, authorized, audited fetch, full stop. This is the identical class of error F236 already found and corrected in `VRS-F005`, left uncaught here because `VRS-F006` was drafted to the same pre-`F199` assumptions and never re-swept once the architecture pivoted.

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 23 September 2026.**
1. The NFR bullet is corrected to: "RateCard and RateCardLine are Tier 1 — fetched on demand by Owner, Finance Admin and HR Admin roles through the permission interceptor, and never cached or persisted on any device."
2. The System States table's "Aged out" row is corrected to "Not applicable. Every read is fetched fresh; nothing is cached on-device to age out of" — the same answer `VRS-F005` already gives its own Tier 2 Contextual Intelligence Panel content.
3. The Depends-On line's "Tier 1 sync and encryption" is corrected to "Tier 1 fetch-on-demand and encryption."

**Status: closed.** Stage 14 is unblocked; nothing in the brief implies building a sync or replication mechanism for RateCard/RateCardLine that `F199`'s architecture does not have.


### F245 — `rateCard.update` cannot express the update Stage 14 requires and has no stale-state contract

Raised 23 September 2026 while tracing the corrected Stage 14 brief (`FDN-121`) into the named-mutation registry, before any product code was written.

**The gap.** The authoritative API contract and Stage 14 Do item 1 both name `rateCard.update(rateCardId, lines)`. The same Do item then said `currency` is caller-supplied on both create and update, while the editor specification presents both name and currency as editable fields — but the update contract's own signature accepts neither. Separately, marking the prior card `is_active: false` on supersession carries no caller base version, even though `VPS-A003` A003-T54 requires one for every state transition and the brief said to mirror `calendar.update`'s supersession pattern; and G02 states the prior node is never edited in place, which the `is_active` change appears to contradict without an explicit exception.

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 23 September 2026.**
1. **`rateCard.update` gains `expectedVersion`, on `calendar.update`'s own precedent (F228).** New signature: `rateCard.update(rateCardId, expectedVersion, lines) -> { newRateCardId }`. `validate()` checks `expectedVersion` against the current card's version first — `stale-state` before anything else — then creates the new version. This is the same version-first ordering F228 already gave `calendar.update`, `holiday.cancel`, `pattern.set` and `pattern.clear`.
2. **`name` and `currency` are never accepted by `update`.** The Stage 14 brief's "caller-supplied on update" claim was a drafting error, not a genuine spec ambiguity — `VRS-F006`'s own API contract never gave `update` a `currency` or `name` parameter, and its "Updating a rate card" user flow only ever describes editing rates, never renaming a card or changing its currency. The new version copies both fields unchanged from the prior card. `currency` remains caller-supplied on `create` only.
3. **The prior card's `is_active: false` transition is a permitted lifecycle-only exception to G02**, not a violation of it. Precedent already exists in this exact codebase: Stage 12's `WorkingPattern` supersession sets the prior pattern's `lifecycle_status: "Superseded"` without touching its substantive fields (`packages/graph/src/mutators/foundation.ts`). G02 protects a card's content — name, currency, lines — from being edited in place; the version-tracking lifecycle flag is the one field every superseding pattern in this codebase is permitted to change on the prior node.

`VRS-F006`'s API contracts, the Rate resolution section and G02 are corrected to state this explicitly, with the version-check requirement recorded as a new G08.

**Status: closed.** Stage 14 is unblocked; `rateCard.update` has one signature, a defined stale-state check, and an explicit statement of which fields carry forward.


### F246 — Card-bearing `assignment.create` has no defined offline or optimistic behavior

Raised 23 September 2026 in the same Stage 14 contract trace, before any product code was written.

**The gap.** Stage 13's `assignment.create` is Tier 0, `onlineOnly: false`, with a real optimistic handler. Stage 14's Do item 3 made the same mutation, when `rate_card_id` is supplied, respons­ible for writing a `governed_by` edge to a RateCard that is never present in the device cache (F244), and Do item 2 made it resolve `effective_billing_rate` against a Tier 1 `RateCardLine` the device cannot read. Do item 7 defined optimistic handlers for the three new Assignment-rate mutations but never revisited `assignment.create`'s own existing handler, whose authoritative effect item 2 had just changed.

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 23 September 2026.**
1. **The `governed_by` edge is written exclusively server-side**, in the mutation's `apply()` step against Postgres — never by any optimistic handler. Nothing on-device ever needs to read this edge: RateCard/RateCardLine are never cached (F244), the local Bench Forecast query needs no rate data at all, and `rateCard.usageCount`/the version-history count are server-only reads (F243). With no local consumer, there is no reason for the optimistic path to attempt a speculative edge write at all — resolving the question without inventing a "dangling edge" mechanism.
2. **`assignment.create`'s optimistic handler is unchanged from Stage 13.** It already writes `rate_card_id` as a plain scalar field and always sets `effective_billing_rate` to the employee's cached `billing_rate_default`, regardless of whether a card is supplied — this was already Stage 13's exact behavior, before `rate_card_id` had any real meaning, and it remains correct now: a deliberate placeholder, corrected by the server's authoritative response once the mutation confirms — the same "deliberately server-only, no client precompute" discipline F224 already established for the 100% capacity constraint and F241 already established for disclosure control.
3. **No conditional `onlineOnly` mechanism is needed.** `assignment.create` stays `onlineOnly: false` and fully offline-capable, card or no card — `MutationDefinition.onlineOnly`'s static, per-name shape needs no change, and card selection is not split out into a separate later step.

`VRS-F006`'s Technical Architecture and Stage 14's brief (Do items 2, 3 and 7) are corrected to state this explicitly, as a new G09.

**Status: closed.** Stage 14 is unblocked; `assignment.create`'s existing offline behavior needs no change, and the new edge write has one clear owner.


### F247 — RateCardLine has no traversable or indexed path from its RateCard

Raised 23 September 2026 while resuming Stage 14 (`FDN-121`) after F245 and F246 were ruled, before any product code was written.

**The gap.** `VRS-F006` makes `RateCardLine` a fixed Tier 1 node whose substantive fields — `rate_card_id`, `seniority_level`, `hourly_rate`, `daily_rate`, `monthly_rate` — live entirely inside the protected partition. Stage 14 requires two point lookups against those fields: `rateCard.getPreview(rateCardId, seniority)` and `resolveAssignmentRate`, which must find the line matching an Assignment's `rate_card_id` and its Employee's `seniority_level`. The spec said RateCardLine carries no edge of its own because nothing needs to traverse *backward* from a RateCard except through its own lines — true, but it never defined how the *forward* lookup those two callers need actually reaches a line. `services/api/src/protected/read.ts`'s `readProtected` accepts only `nodeIds`, filtered by `inArray(ownerId, ids)` — a point lookup by id, never a predicate over decrypted content — so without an id to ask for, neither caller has an address. The only alternative, enumerating every RateCardLine in the workspace and decrypting each to filter in memory, is exactly the implicit foreign-key scan A002-T03 prohibits, the same rule F243 was closed on for Assignment-side `rate_card_id`.

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 23 September 2026.**
1. **`RateCardLine.line_id` is generated deterministically from `(rate_card_id, seniority_level)`, not as a random UUID v4.** This extends a mechanism this codebase already has, not a new one: `services/api/src/auth/membership-edge-ids.ts`'s `deterministicUuid(seed)` (SHA-256 of a seed string, version/variant nibbles forced, still UUID v4-shaped) already derives `membership_of`/`membership_in` edge ids this way, and `packages/schema/src/mutations/foundation.ts` already derives F233's `initialCalendarId`/`initialCalendarEdgeId` the same way. `line_id` becomes `deterministicUuid(\`rate_card_line:${rateCardId}:${seniorityLevel}\`)`, computed server-side in `rateCard.create`/`rateCard.update`'s `apply()` step when each line is written. It remains UUID v4-shaped, satisfying A002-T02 exactly as the existing precedent does.
