2. **`rateCard.getPreview` and `resolveAssignmentRate` compute the same id from context they already have — `rateCardId` and a `seniority_level` — and call `readProtected` with that one node id.** This is a point lookup, not a scan: no new edge, no index over encrypted content, no enumeration. A seniority level with no line under that card computes an id that was never written, so the lookup returns nothing — the exact "resolution falls through" behavior the spec already describes.
3. **This also makes G05 mechanical rather than a rule the mutation must separately enforce.** Two lines for the same seniority under the same card, whether in one `lines` array or across repeated writes to the same version, always compute the identical `line_id` and overwrite the same protected fragment — "a duplicate replaces rather than doubles" falls out of the id scheme itself, with no separate duplicate-detection code required.
4. **Nothing else about RateCardLine changes.** No edge is added (the spec's own "nothing needs to traverse backward" reasoning still holds — this is a forward lookup, not a reverse one); no index over encrypted content is added; RateCard/RateCardLine's Tier 1, Finance-restricted classification (G01) is untouched; `graph_nodes` still carries RateCardLine's ordinary Tier 0 shell row, unchanged, since `readProtected` already requires one to exist for any node it reads.

`VRS-F006`'s Technical Architecture (the RateCardLine block) and API contracts are corrected to state the deterministic id explicitly, as a new G10.

**Status: closed.** Stage 14 is unblocked; `rateCard.getPreview` and `resolveAssignmentRate` both have a real, scan-free lookup path, and G05 is enforced by construction.


### F248 — VRS-F007's Depends-On section omits its real forward dependency on VRS-F028's unbuilt OpenRole

Raised 23 September 2026 while checking `VRS-F007` directly against the code before writing the Stage 15 brief.

**The gap.** `VRS-F007`'s Depends-On line names `VRS-F002`, `VRS-F005`, `VPS-A002`, `VPS-A003` and `VPS-A004` but not `VRS-F028`. Its own text nonetheless gives Ghost creation "an optional link to an existing OpenRole," its API contracts include `ghostResource.linkOpenRole(ghostId, openRoleId)`, and the registered `placeholder_for` edge (GhostResource to OpenRole, confirmed in `packages/schema/src/registry/edges.ts`) exists specifically to carry that link. `OpenRole` itself is already a registered node type (`packages/schema/src/registry/nodes.ts`, owner `VRS-F028`, Tier 0) but has no field schema, no mutation and no create path anywhere in the repository — confirmed by a repository-wide search. `VRS-001`'s Feature Register places `VRS-F028` (F17) well after `VRS-F007` (F04) in MVP order, so no `OpenRole` node can exist by the time Stage 15 ships. This is structurally the same shape as F239: a spec's dependency list reads as complete and unconditional while a real, later-feature-owned prerequisite goes unnamed.

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 23 September 2026.** Same treatment as F239, extended rather than reinvented. `ghostResource.create` accepts the optional `openRoleId` and `ghostResource.linkOpenRole` writes the `placeholder_for` edge now — the mechanism is trivial (an edge write against an already-registered pair) and there is no reason to gate it behind `VRS-F028` landing first. What changes is documentation only: `VRS-F007`'s Depends-On section gains a Partial forward dependencies line — "`VRS-F028` supplies OpenRole creation; until it exists, `openRoleId` has no real targets to link, and every Ghost is created without one" — mirroring `VRS-F005`'s own line for `VRS-F006`/`VRS-F007`/`VRS-F009`/`VRS-F010` exactly. Nothing about Stage 15's build changes; this closes a documentation gap, not a code gap.

**Status: closed.**

### F249 — VRS-F007's promotion API names a second, unspecified path with no defined edge-reconciliation mechanics

Raised 23 September 2026 while checking `VRS-F007` directly against the code before writing the Stage 15 brief.

**The gap.** `ghostResource.promote`'s contract is `promote(ghostId, { fullName, email, employmentType, startDate } | { existingEmployeeId })`. The "Promoting to a real employee" flow names the `existingEmployeeId` branch as "the common case being a candidate hired through `VRS-F028`" — i.e. linking the Ghost to a separate, already-existing Employee node created independently, not through this Ghost's own atomic creation. But G01 fixes the Ghost's own Employee node permanently at creation ("linked permanently by `ghost_employee_id`, set once and never changed"), the promotion transaction as specified "modifies the linked Employee atomically" (singular, the Ghost's own node), and "every Assignment edge, skill edge and piece of graph history is preserved untouched" — none of which says what happens to the Ghost's own placeholder Employee node, or to its Assignment/skill edges, when promotion instead targets a different, pre-existing Employee node. No acceptance criterion exercises this branch; none of G01 through G08 mentions it. Filling it in would mean inventing a cross-node edge-migration mechanism — closing the Ghost's edges and reopening equivalent ones on the target node — that has no precedent anywhere in this codebase, on no more grounding than the phrase "the common case."

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 23 September 2026.** Stage 15 builds the fully-specified path only: `{ fullName, email, employmentType, startDate }`, which every acceptance criterion, G06 and G07 describe in full — the Ghost's own linked Employee node is filled in place, no second node is involved, nothing needs reconciling. The `existingEmployeeId` variant is deferred rather than invented; `ghostResource.promote` validates its input as the single-branch shape only for now, and a caller who passes `existingEmployeeId` receives a clear "not yet supported" rejection rather than a half-built merge. `VRS-F007`'s API contracts and "Promoting to a real employee" flow gain a note recording the deferral, and Out of Scope gains a line naming it, so the gap is documented rather than silently absent.

**Status: closed.**

### F250 — Ghost lifecycle transitions omit the required base version, and promotion's stated guard is not concurrency-safe

Raised 23 September 2026 while tracing the corrected Stage 15 brief (`FDN-122`) into the mutation registry, before any product code was written.

**The gap.** `VRS-F007` and the Stage 15 brief define `ghostResource.cancel(ghostId)` and `ghostResource.promote(ghostId, { fullName, email, employmentType, startDate })`. Both operations transition `GhostResource.lifecycle_status` (`Active -> Canceled` and `Active -> Promoted`), but neither signature carries the base version the person acted against. That directly contradicts `VPS-A003` A003-T54: "Every state-transition mutation MUST carry the base `version` it was decided against, and the server MUST reject it with `stale-state` if the record's version has changed." The mutation registry encodes the same rule in `MutationDefinition.stateTransition`, and every current `stateTransition: true` definition carries `expected_version`, including `entity.deactivate` (F223), `holiday.cancel`/`calendar.update`/`pattern.set`/`pattern.clear` (F228), `assignment.cancel` (F240), and `rateCard.update` (F245).

Promotion has a second, inseparable concurrency gap. The brief says the ordinary lifecycle guard gives G07's exclusivity "for free": the first request sees `Active`, writes `Promoted`, and the second sees `Promoted`. That is true only for sequential requests. `services/api/src/mutations/pipeline.ts` builds a mutation plan by reading current state and uses the database's default transaction isolation unless the mutation name is explicitly placed on its serializable list. Two concurrent promotion transactions can therefore both read `Active`, both pass the guard, and both attempt the Employee update and `promoted_to` write. No Ghost mutation exists on the serializable list yet, and the brief explicitly says no new concurrency mechanism is needed. The promised one-winner behavior is consequently not defined by the contract as written.

The two requirements also leave rejection precedence unresolved. A003-T54 says a changed base version **must** reject as `stale-state`. G07 and the Stage 15 report checklist say every subsequent promotion must reject with a message naming who promoted the Ghost and when, using the Ghost's provenance. A request that loses a concurrent promotion race satisfies both conditions. The brief does not say whether it returns a generic `stale-state`, an actor-and-time `already promoted` reason, or a single structured rejection that satisfies both; choosing one in code would invent product behavior.

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 23 September 2026.** Ruling, extending A003-T54's established pattern (F223/F228/F240/F245) rather than inventing a new one:

1. **Both mutations carry the base version, checked first.** `ghostResource.cancel(ghostId, expectedVersion) -> { success }` and `ghostResource.promote(ghostId, expectedVersion, { fullName, email, employmentType, startDate }) -> { status }` — `stale-state` before anything else, the same shape every other `stateTransition: true` mutation in this codebase already uses.
2. **`ghostResource.promote` joins `services/api/src/mutations/pipeline.ts`'s literal serializable-isolation list**, alongside `calendar.update`, `pattern.set`/`clear`, `assignment.cancel`/`setRateCard` and `rateCard.update` — it is a genuinely multi-step transaction (the GhostResource's own transition, the linked Employee's field update, the `promoted_to` edge write) with exactly the same cross-row-consistency shape those mutations already have, not a single-row update. A transaction that loses the serializable race receives a `40001` and is retried by the pipeline's existing retry loop against fresh state — the same machinery already built for every mutation on that list — so the losing request's version check then correctly sees the new, `Promoted` state. `ghostResource.cancel` is **not** added to that list: unlike promotion, it writes exactly one row (the linked Employee is explicitly untouched by cancellation, per the brief), and a version-conditioned single-row update is race-safe under the database's default isolation the same way every other single-row `expected_version` write already is (`holiday.confirm`'s own exemption from the serializable list, cited in Stage 14's brief, is the precedent for the distinction).
3. **Rejection precedence is resolved by making G07's actor-and-time rejection a specialization of A003-T54's `stale-state`, not a second competing check.** `ghostResource.promote`'s version check is the single source of truth: on a version mismatch, it re-reads the GhostResource's current `lifecycle_status` and its `updated_by`/`updated_at` provenance (already recorded on every write per `VPS-A002`'s universal node conventions); if the current status is `Promoted`, it returns a dedicated "already promoted" rejection naming that actor and time (G07); for any other reason the version moved (a `notes` edit, or `Canceled`), it returns the ordinary generic `stale-state` rejection A003-T54 requires. One check, one code path, two rejection messages depending on what it finds.
4. **`ghostResource.cancel`'s optimistic handler supplies the client's cached `expectedVersion`** the same way every other offline-capable `stateTransition` mutation's optimistic handler already does; a server-side stale-state rejection on reconnection is handled by the same generic optimistic-rejection/rollback path every other mutation on this list already has — no new mechanism.

`VRS-F007`'s API contracts are corrected to show both signatures carrying `expectedVersion`. Stage 15's brief is corrected to match.

**Status: closed.** Stage 15 is unblocked.

### F251 — Stage 15's “no permission override” requirement contradicts the Stage 13 policy rows it inherits

Raised 23 September 2026 while resuming Stage 15 (`FDN-122`) on F250's ruling, before any product code was written.

**The gap.** Stage 15 says three times that `GhostResource` and `OpenRole` have no `MATRIX_OVERRIDES` entry: its opening code trace says both already fall through to the default policy class, Do item 9 requires a test proving “zero `MATRIX_OVERRIDES` entry for either,” and the Done criteria require Owner/HR Admin full plus Manager/Team Member read-only “per the existing default class.” The live code says the opposite. `packages/schema/src/policy/policy-table.ts` already contains explicit `GhostResource` and `OpenRole` overrides granting Owner/HR Admin full and Finance Admin/Manager/Team Member read. Stage 13's authoritative brief Do item 8 explicitly required those exact five workspace-staffing rows (`Assignment`, `Project`, `Client`, `GhostResource`, `OpenRole`); commit `f2d928d` added them, the policy test exercises them, the Stage 13 report records them as completed, and the independent review approved the stage.

Deleting the two rows does not make Stage 15's stated test pass. `VPS-A004`'s Standard fallback grants Manager `Full (direct reports)` and Team Member `Read (own + team)`, not unqualified read. The interceptor can resolve those person-scoped qualifiers only for an Employee row (and the separately registered BurnoutAlert subject path); for `GhostResource` and `OpenRole`, `rowScopeSatisfied` returns false. With no explicit overrides, Manager and Team Member therefore receive `none`, not the read-only access Stage 15 requires. The two code paths are materially different, not redundant representations of the same policy.

There is a second product-level conflict in the same decision. `VRS-F007`'s Creating a Ghost flow says an Owner, HR Admin **or Manager** creates one. Both the existing Stage 13 override and Stage 15's Done criterion grant Manager read-only, so the permission interceptor would reject `ghostResource.create` for a Manager. Changing Manager to full would satisfy that user-facing flow but contradict the two already-approved briefs; retaining read-only would require correcting the feature flow. No existing text says which fact wins.

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 23 September 2026.** Ruling, keeping the codebase's existing node-type-level permission model rather than inventing a new one:

1. **Stage 13's `MATRIX_OVERRIDES` rows for `GhostResource` and `OpenRole` are retained, not removed.** They are a real, tested, already-approved policy decision (Stage 13 Do item 8, commit `f2d928d`), not a redundant restatement of the `Standard` default — `rowScopeSatisfied` cannot resolve either node type's Manager/Team-Member cells without them, so deleting them would silently drop Manager and Team Member to `none`, the opposite of Stage 15's own intent. Stage 15's brief is corrected everywhere it claimed the two node types "already fall through to the default policy class" or required a test proving "zero `MATRIX_OVERRIDES` entry" — that claim was simply wrong.
2. **`GhostResource`'s `manager` cell is extended from `READ_ANY()` to `FULL_ANY()`.** This is the one cell that must change: `VRS-F007`'s own Creating-a-Ghost flow names Owner, HR Admin **or Manager** as able to create one, and `authorizeWrite` requires a literal `"full"` outcome for any write — there is no finer-grained "can create, can't otherwise write" permission tier anywhere in this codebase (confirmed by an exhaustive grep across every mutation/permission file for a per-mutation role gate; none exists). Read-only Manager access, as Stage 15's Done criteria wrongly required, makes the feature's own primary creation flow unbuildable. `team-member` stays `READ_ANY()` — no text anywhere gives Team Member a create/write role for Ghosts. `OpenRole`'s override is untouched in both cells; Stage 15 never writes an `OpenRole` node, only the `placeholder_for` edge from `GhostResource`.
3. **Manager's resulting full access to `ghostResource.promote`/`cancel` is accepted, not a second gap.** This codebase's permission model is node-type-level, one cell per role per node type — there is no mechanism to grant "full" for one mutation on a node type and "read" for another, and none has ever been built across fourteen prior stages. `VRS-F007`'s own Security Considerations already state Ghost Resources "carry no elevated privacy concern" and that promotion is "a data-integrity concern rather than a confidentiality one," audited per `VPS-F004` regardless of actor. Building a first-of-its-kind per-mutation role gate for this one case would be disproportionate to a risk the feature's own spec already disclaims, and no existing precedent supports it. If a narrower rule is ever wanted, it is scoped and built as its own feature, not invented ad hoc here.
4. **`policy-table.test.ts`'s shared `it.each(["Assignment", "Project", "Client", "GhostResource", "OpenRole"])` test is split.** `GhostResource` gets its own dedicated test (`manager: "full"`, `"team-member": "read"`, `owner`/`hr-admin: "full"`); `Assignment`, `Project`, `Client` and `OpenRole` keep the original shared assertions unchanged, since none of their cells change.

`VRS-F007` gains a Security Considerations note recording that Manager's write access is node-type-scoped, not mutation-scoped, and that this is a deliberate, accepted consequence of the existing permission model. Stage 15's brief is corrected to match all four points.

**Status: closed.** Stage 15 is unblocked.

### F252 — Ghost promotion requires a successful audit event that VPS-F004 does not define

Raised 23 September 2026 while resuming Stage 15 (`FDN-122`) on F251's ruling, before any product code was written.

**The gap.** `VRS-F007`'s Security Considerations say promotion is audited per `VPS-F004` because the actor and timing matter. Stage 15 Do item 5 is more specific: “reuse the existing `appendAudit` call already wired into every named mutation's `apply()` step,” adding no event type, operation or target. The live code has no such per-mutation call. `services/api/src/mutations/pipeline.ts` delegates audit behavior to `authorizeWrite`; the interceptor audits every denial and successful Tier 1/2/3-sensitive operation according to its rules, but deliberately writes no successful Tier 0 audit entry. Both the GhostResource transition and the linked Employee's operational update are Tier 0.

`VPS-F004` confirms the omission is intentional rather than an implementation oversight. Its “What is logged” section says every denial is logged, while successful-access logging covers Tier 1 and Tier 3 only; G04 repeats that closed rule. Its G02 says AuditEntry creation happens exclusively inside `VPS-A004`'s interceptor and no feature creates one. The live audit schema's closed event taxonomy contains `PermissionDenied`, `SensitiveAccessGranted`, `AuthorizedOperationFailed`, `PrivilegedProjectionAuthorized` and `CryptographicErasureExecuted`. None represents a successful ordinary Tier 0 conversion or lifecycle transition. Reusing `SensitiveAccessGranted` would misclassify a Tier 0 write as sensitive access and violate the event's schema meaning; adding a direct feature-level `appendAudit` call would violate G02 and the architecture check's centralized-writer rule.

This is the same class of taxonomy gap F221 and F225 exposed: a feature requires an audit fact that the authoritative audit vocabulary does not represent. Here it cannot be silently omitted because `VRS-F007` and the Stage 15 brief explicitly make the success audit part of the promotion contract.

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 23 September 2026, on F221 and F225's own precedent.** Both already resolved the identical shape of gap — a feature spec claiming a `VPS-F004`-audited event for an ordinary, permitted Tier 0 write that the closed taxonomy has no category for — by pointing at provenance the graph already carries, not by extending the taxonomy. The same reasoning applies here without modification:

1. **`VPS-F004`'s closed audit taxonomy is not extended, and no feature-level `appendAudit` call is added anywhere in Stage 15.** Ghost promotion is an ordinary, permitted Tier 0 write — not a denial, not a Tier 1/3 access grant, not an operation failure, not a privileged projection, not an erasure. Reusing an existing event type would misrepresent it (F252's own gap report already rules this out for `SensitiveAccessGranted`); adding a new one for a single feature's ordinary write is exactly what F221 declined to do for Entity transfer and F225 declined to do for Holiday confirmation. `VPS-F004` G02 (interceptor-only creation) and G04 (Tier 1/3-only success logging) are both respected unmodified.
2. **The underlying need — who promoted this Ghost, and when — is already met structurally, with no additional write.** `VPS-A002`'s Universal Node Conventions make `updated_by`/`updated_at` mandatory on every node type except the two named exceptions (`AuditEntry`, the two Aggregate-contribution types); `GhostResource` and Employee are neither, so both already carry them. When `ghostResource.promote` transitions `GhostResource.lifecycle_status` to `Promoted`, `GhostResource.updated_by`/`updated_at` records who and when. `VRS-F007`'s own G03 already establishes `promoted_to` is "created exactly once, at promotion, never modified" — the identical permanent-edge-history shape F221 relied on for `scoped_to_entity`'s transfer record — and `VPS-A002`'s Rule 2 ("edges carry provenance") makes its `created_by`/`created_at` mandatory too. The linked Employee node's own `updated_by`/`updated_at` records the same write on the employment-record side. Together these already answer exactly what `VRS-F007`'s Security Considerations wanted a `VPS-F004` entry for.
3. **`VRS-F007`'s Security Considerations bullet "Promotion is audited per `VPS-F004`" is corrected in place**, the same correction F221 made to `VRS-F003` and F225 made to `VRS-F004`, to instead name the structural provenance above. Stage 15's Do item 5 ("reuse the existing `appendAudit` call... no new event type") is removed outright — there is no `appendAudit` call to reuse or add.

**Status: closed.** Stage 15 is unblocked.

### F253 — Ghost creation cannot satisfy Employee's required fields, and promotion never supplies an employee code

Raised 23 September 2026 while resuming Stage 15 (`FDN-122`) on F252's ruling, before any product code was written.

**The gap.** Stage 15 Do item 1 says the Ghost's linked Employee node uses `employeeOperationalRecord`'s existing shape, with `employee_type: "Ghost"`, `job_title`, `start_date`, Active status, optional seniority/rate, and “every other field null.” `VRS-F007`'s Technical Architecture states the same rule. The helper cannot construct that record: its `EmployeeCreateFields` input requires `employee_code`, `full_name`, `email`, `job_title`, `employment_type` and `start_date`, and it always writes required `contracted_hours` (default 40). `VRS-F002`'s canonical Employee schema independently declares `employee_code`, `full_name`, `email`, `job_title`, `employment_type`, `start_date` and `contracted_hours` required for every Employee; only `employee_type` distinguishes a Ghost.

No caller value exists for four of those required facts. `ghostResource.create` accepts role title, projected start, optional seniority/skills/rate/OpenRole — no employee code, name, email, employment type or contracted hours. Synthesizing placeholders would invent human-readable identity and uniqueness facts the specification says remain null; passing null cannot typecheck through `employeeOperationalRecord` and contradicts `VRS-F002`'s schema. This is not merely a creation-time issue: Do item 4's promotion details supply full name, email, employment type and start date, but still no `employee_code`. After a successful promotion the node would have `employee_type: "Employee"` while its required workspace-unique human-readable employee code remained absent or fabricated. Non-FullTime contracted hours are also specified by `VRS-F002` G07 as prompted rather than defaulted, but promotion provides no value.

The live graph's generic node parser does not validate feature fields, so an implementation could technically persist an invalid Employee record. That would hide the contract defect rather than resolve it: downstream code and future imports are entitled to rely on `VRS-F002`'s required-field schema, and Stage 15 explicitly directs both server and optimistic client to share one canonical record constructor.

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 23 September 2026.** A Ghost-specific record constructor, kept entirely separate from the real-Employee path Stage 8 already built and approved:

1. **A second, narrower schema and constructor pair, for Ghosts only.** `packages/schema/src/employee.ts` gains `ghostEmployeeCreateFieldsSchema` (`job_title`, `start_date` required; `seniority_level`, `billing_rate_default` optional — exactly `ghostResource.create`'s own input set, nothing else, `.strict()`) and `ghostEmployeeOperationalRecord(fields)`, returning the same record shape `employeeOperationalRecord` already returns but with `employee_type: "Ghost"`, and `employee_code`, `full_name`, `email` and `employment_type` explicitly `null` — the four genuinely unknowable identity/employment facts, matching the discriminated-schema pattern this codebase already uses elsewhere (`packages/schema/src/audit.ts`, `services/api/src/permission/principal.ts`). `employeeCreateFieldsSchema` and `employeeOperationalRecord` are untouched — Stage 8's real-Employee contract keeps its existing required fields exactly as approved.
2. **`contracted_hours` is not treated as an identity fact.** `ghostEmployeeOperationalRecord` defaults it to 40, the same default `employeeOperationalRecord` already applies when a real `employee.create` call omits it (`VRS-F002` G07's "prompted rather than defaulted" is, as built, a UI-only convention with no server-side enforcement anywhere in this codebase — Stage 15 has no UI, so it inherits exactly the same non-enforcement, not a new gap). The Bench Forecast needs a real number for a Ghost's assumed capacity; 40 is the same reasonable default a real employee gets.
3. **Promotion's contract gains `employeeCode` (required) and `contractedHours` (optional).** There is no employee-code auto-generation mechanism anywhere in this codebase — `employee.create` already requires the caller to supply it — so promotion asks for the same human decision real hiring already asks for. `contractedHours`, if given, overwrites the Ghost's current value; if omitted, the value already on the node (40 by default, or whatever it already holds) carries forward unchanged, the same "every other field untouched" principle G06 already states for Assignment/skill edges and graph history.
4. **Promotion validates uniqueness with the mutation layer's own existing helper.** `services/api/src/mutations/employee.ts` already exports `requireUnique(ctx, fields, excludeNodeId?)`, used by `employee.create` for exactly this check. Promotion calls it identically — `requireUnique(ctx, { email, employee_code: employeeCode }, employeeId)`, excluding the Ghost's own linked Employee node since this is an update to that node, not a new one — returning the same `duplicate-email`/`duplicate-code` rejections `employee.create` already returns. No new uniqueness mechanism is invented.
5. **`VRS-F007`'s "every other field null until promotion" line is corrected** to name the four fields precisely (`employee_code`, `full_name`, `email`, `employment_type`) rather than "every other field," since `contracted_hours` is not one of them. Its API contracts, Technical Architecture and Stage 15's Do items 1 and 4 are corrected to match.

**Status: closed.** Stage 15 is unblocked.

### F254 — `promoted_to` has no governing Employee partition, so no Ghost can be promoted

Raised 24 September 2026 during Stage 15 implementation (`FDN-122`) when the first real `ghostResource.promote` integration test reached the server permission interceptor.

**The gap.** `promoted_to` is registered as `GhostResource → Employee` in `packages/schema/src/registry/edges.ts`, but it declares no `governingPartitions` entry. Employee is tier-split into `operational` and `compensation`. `services/api/src/permission/interceptor.ts`'s `edgeRoleDecision` deliberately refuses an edge write with `reason: "role"` whenever a split endpoint has no reviewed governing partition; it does so before considering the caller's role. The real Stage 15 integration test therefore reaches the authoritative interceptor and rejects promotion for HR Admin, and the same structural check rejects Owner too. The GhostResource and linked Employee node updates never begin.

This is the same class of omission F208 found for `managed_by`/`scoped_to_entity` and F235 found for `pattern_for`: the endpoint pair is structurally registered, but it is not writable through the server-authoritative permission layer until the split endpoint's governing partition is specified. Stage 15's opening trace says the Ghost node and both edges are “already fully registered” and Do item 4 requires the `promoted_to` write, but neither the brief, `VRS-F007`, nor `VPS-A002` assigns that edge to Employee's `operational` or `compensation` partition.

The surrounding evidence points strongly to `operational`: promotion changes only Employee's Tier 0 operational identity fields, `VRS-F007` explicitly calls promotion an ordinary Tier 0 write (F252), and `has_skill`, `managed_by`, `scoped_to_entity`, `pattern_for`, `assignment_of`, and `holds_certification` already declare `{ Employee: "operational" }` for comparable operational relationships. But selecting a governing partition is an authorization decision. F208 and F235 both required that decision to be recorded in `VPS-A002` before code widened the relationship's write reach, so Stage 15 must not silently infer it here.

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 24 September 2026, on F208 and F235's own precedent.** The evidence the finding itself lays out is conclusive and not a close call: promotion changes only Employee's Tier 0 operational identity fields (F252 already established promotion is an ordinary Tier 0 write, not a Tier 1/2/3 one); `VRS-F007` never touches compensation; and six existing edges targeting a split Employee endpoint — `has_skill`, `holds_certification`, `assignment_of`, `managed_by`, `scoped_to_entity`, `pattern_for` — are all already declared `{ Employee: "operational" }` for exactly this reason. No evidence anywhere points toward `compensation`.

1. **`promoted_to` is declared `{ Employee: "operational" }`** in `packages/schema/src/registry/edges.ts`'s `promoted_to` edge group, the same one-line addition F208 made for `managed_by`/`scoped_to_entity` and F235 made for `pattern_for`. No other change to the edge registry is needed.
2. **`VPS-A002`'s governing-partitions table gains a `promoted_to` row**, immediately after `pattern_for`'s, following the same format and rationale style as the six existing Employee-operational entries.
3. **`VRS-F007`'s Technical Architecture gains a one-line note** recording the governing partition, the same way `VPS-A002`'s own text already treats this as a reviewed, recorded authorization decision rather than an inferred one.
4. **No change to Stage 15's Do item 4 or Done criteria beyond registering the partition** — the already-written real-Postgres integration test (`dae1d01`) was correctly built against the intended behavior; it now exercises a permission layer that actually authorizes the write instead of structurally refusing it before any role is even considered.

**Status: closed.** Stage 15 is unblocked; the preserved implementation at `dae1d01` needs only this one registry declaration to proceed.


### F255 — `Assignment.manager` is scoped to `READ_ANY()`, contradicting `VPS-A004`'s own Permission Matrix

Raised 24 September 2026 checking `VRS-F008` directly against the code before writing the Stage 16 brief, since the feature's override gate assumes a Manager can already write Assignment.

**The gap.** `VPS-A004`'s own authoritative Permission Matrix table states `Assignment | Full | Full | Read | Full | Read` for Owner, HR Admin, Finance Admin, Manager and Team Member in that order — an unscoped Full for Manager. But `packages/schema/src/policy/policy-table.ts`'s `MATRIX_OVERRIDES` declares `Assignment.manager: READ_ANY()`. `git blame` isolates that single line to Stage 13's `f2d928d` (23 September 2026); every other line in the same `Assignment` block — `owner`, `hr-admin`, `finance-admin`, `team-member` — traces to the original interceptor bootstrap commit `67ad2c1c` (21 September 2026). Stage 13's own brief and completion report never claimed to narrow Assignment's Manager row, and no finding raised during Stage 13 discusses it; this is an unreviewed regression against the suite's own table, caught only now because `VRS-F008` is the first feature to actually exercise a Manager write to Assignment. Under the shipped code, a Manager cannot call `assignment.create`, `assignment.update`, or any new Stage 16 mutation — `conflictResolution.overrideAndProceed` included — because the interceptor refuses the write before the feature's own authorization check (caller must be the employee's manager or an Owner) ever runs.

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 24 September 2026.** `packages/schema/src/policy/policy-table.ts`'s `Assignment.manager` cell is corrected from `READ_ANY()` to `FULL_ANY()`, matching `VPS-A004`'s table exactly — unscoped Full, not a `direct-reports`-scoped `cell(...)` entry, since the table specifies no such scoping for this row. No other cell in the `Assignment` block changes. This is a one-line code fix, a Do item for Codex rather than a documentation-only correction, since `VPS-A004`'s table was already correct and the code is what drifted from it.

**Status: closed.**

### F256 — `validateCapacity` aggregates calendar days, contradicting `VRS-F008`'s own G02 and its "reused, not reimplemented" claim

Raised 24 September 2026 checking `VRS-F005`'s existing hard constraint against `VRS-F008`'s Technical Architecture section before writing the Stage 16 brief.

**The gap.** `VRS-F008`'s Technical Architecture section states the conflict computation is "exactly the aggregation [[VRS-F005]]'s constraint already performs, reused rather than reimplemented," and G02 requires "Overlap is measured in working days per [[VRS-F004]]. Assignments sharing only non-working days do not conflict." But `services/api/src/mutations/assignment.ts`'s existing `validateCapacity` — called from both `assignmentCreate` and `assignmentUpdate` — aggregates via `eachDate(from, to)`, a plain calendar-day iterator with no reference to `VRS-F004`'s calendar, holidays or patterns at all; it walks every calendar day from `startDate` to `endDate` inclusive. Reusing this function unmodified for the new `conflictCheck.evaluate`, exactly as the spec's own words direct, would carry calendar-day aggregation straight into new code G02 explicitly forbids: two assignments overlapping only on a weekend or public holiday would register a false conflict or an inflated combined total.

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 24 September 2026.** Extended, not replaced, on F237/G08's own precedent. `resolveWorkingDay` (`packages/schema/src/working-day.ts`, F237's pure, portable implementation) and `resolvedDayOn` (`services/api/src/graph/working-days.ts`, G08's tx-aware wrapper) are already the one established mechanism for this exact resolution, currently called only from `services/api/src/permission/bench-forecast-queries.ts`'s three read-query call sites, each behind a `readGuard`. `validateCapacity`'s date loop gains a working-day filter through this same mechanism, using the mutation's own already-granted `tx`/`principal` context in place of a query module's `readGuard`, at both of its existing call sites — a genuine, small behavior correction to already-shipped `VRS-F005` code, not new code. `VRS-F005`'s own spec gains a one-line note recording the working-day filter. The new `conflictCheck.evaluate` read query and `conflictResolution.sweepOvercommitted` reuse the now-corrected `validateCapacity` (or a shared aggregation helper extracted from it) directly rather than reimplementing the overlap sum a second time, making "reused, not reimplemented" true of the shipped code rather than only of the prose.

**Status: closed.**

### F257 — `VRS-F008`'s "audited per `VPS-F004`" claim has no home in the closed five-value audit taxonomy

Raised 24 September 2026, the identical shape F221, F225 and F252 already ruled on.

**The gap.** `VRS-F008`'s Security Considerations states "Overrides are audited per [[VPS-F004]]." `VPS-F004`'s own closed five-value taxonomy — `PermissionDenied`, `SensitiveAccessGranted`, `AuthorizedOperationFailed`, `PrivilegedProjectionAuthorized`, `CryptographicErasureExecuted` — has no category matching an ordinary, successful, already-authorized Tier-0 write, which is exactly the shape of `conflictResolution.overrideAndProceed`: a caller already permitted to write Assignment (the employee's manager or an Owner, per F255's corrected policy) performing a standard mutation that happens to carry a reason. Inventing a sixth taxonomy value would mean amending `VPS-F004`'s closed enum, which F221 and F225 already ruled against for materially identical claims, and which F252 ruled against for Ghost Resources' promotion.

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 24 September 2026, on F221/F225/F252's own precedent.** No `appendAudit` call is added for `overrideAndProceed`. The override is fully accounted for by the write itself: `capacity_override_reason`, `capacity_override_by` and `capacity_override_at` are already-scaffolded fields on Assignment (confirmed present and null-initialized in `assignmentCreate`'s `apply()`), visible to anyone who could already see the Assignment per the same Security Considerations section's own second bullet, and covered by `VPS-A002`'s mandatory Universal Node Conventions the same way F252 relied on for Ghost promotion. `VRS-F008`'s Security Considerations bullet is corrected to describe this — the override is recorded on the record itself, not separately audited — matching F221/F225/F252's own corrected wording pattern.

**Status: closed.**

### F258 — `VRS-F008`'s Depends-On section omits its real forward dependency on `VPS-A006`'s unbuilt job-scheduling infrastructure

Raised 24 September 2026, the identical shape F248 already ruled on.

**The gap.** `VRS-F008`'s Depends-On line names `VRS-F002`, `VRS-F004`, `VRS-F005`, `VRS-F007` and `VPS-A004`, but not `VPS-A006` — the architecture document that is the actual authority for the "four-hourly cadence" the safety-net sweep's own prose invokes twice ("on the same four-hourly cadence [[VRS-F012]] uses"). `VPS-A006`'s own Job queue and scheduling section names both `VRS-F012`'s bench sweep and `VRS-F008`'s own capacity safety-net sweep, by name, as part of the reason BullMQ (workers in `services/jobs`) was chosen over a Postgres-based alternative — a real, already-decided dependency, not an invented one. But no `services/jobs` directory and no BullMQ reference exists anywhere in the repository, confirmed by a repository-wide search, and `VRS-F012` itself — the sibling feature whose cadence this sweep claims to reuse — sits four stages later in `VRS-001`'s build order (Stage 20) and is not yet built either. Read alone, "the same four-hourly cadence [[VRS-F012]] uses" suggests an existing schedule Codex could simply hook into; in fact neither the schedule nor its infrastructure exists yet for anyone. This is structurally the same shape as F248: a spec's dependency list reads as complete while a real, later-owned prerequisite goes unnamed.

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 24 September 2026, on F248's own Partial-forward-dependency precedent.** G06 already scopes the sweep correctly as pure product logic — "The sweep writes nothing. It is a read query" — and that is exactly what Stage 16 builds: `conflictResolution.sweepOvercommitted` ships as a complete, directly callable, fully tested read query now. What Stage 16 does not build is the periodic BullMQ trigger that calls it every four hours; that belongs to `VPS-A006`'s own not-yet-built `services/jobs` decision, shared with `VRS-F012` and six other named features, and gating Stage 16 on it would mean building unrelated platform infrastructure inside a Core feature's stage. `VRS-F008`'s Depends-On section gains a Partial forward dependency line naming `VPS-A006`, mirroring F248's own line for `VRS-F007`/`VRS-F028` exactly, and the safety-net sweep's own prose gains a note that the read query is complete and callable on demand from launch, with periodic scheduling deferred to whichever stage builds `VPS-A006`'s job queue.

**Status: closed.** Stage 16 is unblocked; the sweep's own scheduling is deferred as a named, non-blocking gap rather than built or silently ignored.

### F259 — Stage 16's server-only execution contract contradicts `VRS-F008`'s local and offline guarantees

Raised 24 September 2026 while tracing the corrected Stage 16 brief into the existing server-authoritative mutation and device-cache boundaries, before any product code was written.

**The gap.** `VRS-F008` makes two execution-boundary promises that the Stage 16 brief explicitly reverses:

1. Its Non-Functional Requirements say `conflictCheck.evaluate` “returns within 100ms from the local graph, before any save is attempted.” The same section says “Computation, panel and override all function fully offline.” The feature's flow depends on that evaluation opening the resolution panel before a save.
2. Stage 16 Do item 4 instead defines `conflictCheck.evaluate` only as a new `services/api/src/permission/conflict-queries.ts` server query. Do item 9 then marks `conflictResolution.overrideAndProceed` `onlineOnly: true`, gives it no optimistic handler, and says an offline caller remains pending until server confirmation because both the manager/Owner gate and corrected capacity total are server-only facts. The stage is expressly server-side only and defines no portable or device-cache evaluator.

These are observable opposite behaviors. A network request cannot guarantee a local-graph sub-100ms result or operate during a network cut. An `onlineOnly` mutation with no optimistic handler cannot complete an override fully offline. Conversely, adding a client-side evaluator and optimistic override would contradict Do item 9's explicit server-confirmation decision and would create a device-side answer to the authorization and capacity questions the corrected brief says are server-only.

The current code does not supply an implicit resolution. `assignment.create` and `assignment.update` have optimistic handlers, but the authoritative capacity validation exists only in `services/api/src/mutations/assignment.ts`; the client handlers do not compute working-day-aware capacity. `resolvedDayOn` is a server graph wrapper, while the portable `resolveWorkingDay` function needs caller-supplied calendar, holiday and pattern facts. No Stage 16 contract defines how the local query assembles those facts, what happens when the permission-filtered device cache lacks one, or how a locally accepted override reconciles with the server-only manager gate.

A ruling must select one execution boundary and correct the other document before implementation. If the server-authoritative brief wins, `VRS-F008`'s local-graph 100ms and fully-offline statements need the same explicit server-confirmed correction used for other post-pivot features. If the feature spec's local/offline contract wins, the brief needs a portable local evaluator, its inputs and failure behavior, and a reconciled override authorization model rather than `onlineOnly`.

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 24 September 2026.** The contradiction is real, but it is the brief that drifted from established precedent, not the spec that is stale — re-reading the actual code this codebase already ships resolves both halves without touching `VRS-F008`'s NFRs at all.

1. **`conflictCheck.evaluate` is a portable local query, not a server query.** `services/api/src/mutations/assignment.ts`'s own `assignmentCreate` optimistic handler already carries this exact, pre-Stage-16 comment: *"Capacity is deliberately server-only: one device may not have another device's newest Assignment yet."* That is a standing architectural decision this stage does not reopen — the local cache can never be the *authoritative* source for the 100% ceiling, full stop. But `conflictCheck.evaluate`'s own NFR ("before any save is attempted," "100ms," "fully offline") is asking for a fast, best-effort, advisory figure for the interactive panel and the soft warning, not a second source of truth — and this codebase already has the exact pattern for that: `packages/graph/src/queries/bench-forecast.ts` is a portable local query reading `localNodes`/`localEdges` from the device's own replicated Tier 0 cache (`packages/graph/src/queries/cache-data.ts`) and calling the same pure `resolveWorkingDay` function `validateCapacity` uses server-side (F256), entirely on-device, no server round-trip. `conflictCheck.evaluate` is built the same way, in a new `packages/graph/src/queries/conflict-check.ts` — not in `services/api/src/permission/conflict-queries.ts` as Stage 16's Do item 4 specified. The server's `validateCapacity` remains the sole *authority*; nothing about its deliberate server-only design changes, and the two never need to agree exactly — the local figure is best-effort by design, the same caveat `assignmentCreate`'s own comment already lives by.
2. **`overrideAndProceed` gets a genuine optimistic handler, not `onlineOnly`.** `assignmentCreate`'s optimistic handler performs *no* capacity check at all, locally or otherwise — it writes the Assignment unconditionally and lets the server's `validateCapacity` reject it afterward via the outbox's existing undo path if the ceiling is actually breached. `overrideAndProceed` is structurally the same write (an Assignment, this time deliberately above the ceiling) plus the override fields the caller already supplies. The manager/Owner gate is not a server-only fact either: `managed_by` is a Tier 0 edge, already locally replicated, and `VRS-F008`'s own Interface Specification already requires the client to resolve it locally regardless ("Where the viewer is not the employee's manager or an Owner, the option does not render"). The optimistic handler writes the Assignment locally exactly as `assignmentCreate`'s does, using the client's own locally-resolved manager/Owner check to decide whether to attempt the write at all; the server re-verifies that same gate authoritatively inside the mutation — never trusting the client's own permission decision, the same as every other permission-enforced mutation in this codebase — and rejects via the same undo path on a stale or wrong local read. No new mechanism is invented; this is `assignmentCreate`'s own pattern, reused, not `ghostResource.promote`'s (that mutation's `onlineOnly` status is for a genuinely different reason — a multi-row serializable transaction and a workspace-wide uniqueness check neither of which applies here).
3. **`conflictResolution.sweepOvercommitted` is unaffected.** Nothing in the spec claims the sweep's own computation runs locally — "the sweep is best-effort and runs on next connectivity" describes the overcommitment list screen showing cached results while offline, not the sweep itself. It stays server-side per F258's own ruling, unchanged.

This resolves F259 without reopening F255–F258. Stage 16's brief (Do items 2, 4, 5, 9, Done criteria, What to check) and `VRS-F008`'s NFR section are corrected to match.

**Status: closed.** Stage 16 is unblocked; `codex/stage-16-capacity-conflict-resolution` resumes on the corrected brief — no branch restart.

**Status: open.** Stage 16 is blocked before product code; implementing either side now would invent which authoritative contract to discard.

### F260 — F259's local authorization checks have no caller-role input

Raised 24 September 2026 while tracing F259's corrected local query and optimistic mutation contracts into the device-cache APIs, before product code was written.

**The gap.** F259 requires two local authorization results, but neither corrected execution contract receives the facts needed to produce them:

1. Stage 16 Do item 4 requires `conflictCheck.evaluate(database, { employeeId, startDate, endDate, billablePercentage, excludeAssignmentId?, nearCapacityWarningThreshold })` to return `callerCanEdit`, resolved from the caller's roles and the employee's active `managed_by` edge. The function input contains no caller user id, caller Employee id or roles. Its prescribed local node set is Assignment, Employee, Project, WorkingCalendar, Holiday and WorkingPattern — no User or WorkspaceMembership fact from which a caller role could be discovered.
2. Do item 9 requires the optimistic `conflictResolution.overrideAndProceed` handler to reject locally unless the caller is the target employee's manager or an Owner. `MutatorContext` in `packages/graph/src/mutators/foundation.ts` carries `cache`, `workspaceId`, `userId`, `mutationId` and `now`; it carries no role or membership identity. `OptimisticCache` in `packages/graph/src/mutators/cache.ts` supports point node/edge access and edge traversal only, with no node enumeration that could discover a WorkspaceMembership record.

The Manager half is mechanically answerable from the existing Tier 0 cache: follow the target Employee's active outgoing `managed_by` edge, read the manager Employee and compare its `user_id` with `MutatorContext.userId`. The Owner half is not. An Owner need not be the target employee's manager, and Owner is a workspace role rather than a fact carried on either Employee node. The query's `callerCanEdit` result has the same problem: F255 restores `Assignment.manager` to unscoped Full, while Owner and HR Admin also have Full, so editability cannot be derived from the target edge alone without the caller's role context.

Silently applying every optimistic override and relying on server rejection would contradict the explicit local gate F259 just added. Rejecting every non-manager would refuse valid Owner overrides. Extending the query arguments and `MutatorContext` with trusted role facts, teaching the cache API how to find membership facts, or redefining the local check as advisory are materially different architecture and product contracts. The corrected brief selects none of them.

A ruling must define how caller identity and role facts enter both local paths, including which source is trusted for an offline optimistic decision. Until then, implementing either local authorization result would invent a contract the specification does not provide.

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 24 September 2026.** Neither half needs a materially different architecture — both are answerable from facts this codebase already carries, or already carries in an equivalent ambient-session context; the corrected brief simply never named the caller-identity inputs, the identical shape of omission F259 itself was.

1. **The Manager half needs no new capability — `OptimisticCache`'s existing point-access surface already answers it.** `services/api/src/permission/interceptor.ts`'s own `rowScopeSatisfied` already resolves an employee's manager the same direction this needs: `outgoing(tx, workspaceId, employeeId, "managed_by", asOf)`, from the *target* employee toward the manager, not from the caller outward. The client-side equivalent needs only operations `OptimisticCache` already exposes — `edgesFrom(employeeId, "managed_by")` for the target employee's outgoing edge, then `getNode(managerId)`, a point lookup, not enumeration, to read the manager Employee's `user_id` and compare it to `context.userId` (already present on `MutatorContext`). No reverse lookup of the caller's own Employee id is needed at all, because the edge already points away from the target toward the manager. The local query (`conflictCheck.evaluate`) does the identical thing over `localNodes`/`localEdges`, the same relationship `bench-forecast.ts` already has with its own local data.
2. **The Owner half needs one small, precedented context extension, not a new mechanism.** `WorkspaceMembership` is Tier 0, Standard protection (`packages/schema/src/registry/nodes.ts`) and already replicates to every device — `packages/graph/src/sync-client/engine.ts` already special-cases a `WorkspaceMembership` row change to invalidate the protected-value cache, proof the client already receives these rows today, simply unused by any caller-identity logic yet. The server's own equivalent context bundle, `CurrentWorkspaceSession` (`services/api/src/auth/workspace-session.ts`), already carries `roles` alongside `userId`/`workspaceId`/`membershipId` as one unit; `MutatorContext` today carries only the `userId`/`workspaceId` half of that same family of facts. `deriveEffectiveRoles` (`packages/schema/src/policy/effective-roles.ts`) is already a pure, portable function exported from `@vulto/schema`, and its own doc comment already anticipates exactly this — being fed "the stored role set... from a device-unlock grant or a role-refresh response," session-level facts the app layer already holds outside the graph cache, the same place `userId` itself already comes from (`InitPayload`, `packages/graph/src/sync-client/protocol.ts`).

Ruled: `MutatorContext` gains `readonly roles: readonly WorkspaceRole[]`, threaded through `InitPayload` and `packages/graph/src/sync-client/engine.ts` the same way `userId` already is — a plain, non-secret role label, not a credential, so `InitPayload`'s own doc comment ("nothing here carries a token: the session cookie travels with the worker's own fetch") stays true. `overrideAndProceed`'s optimistic handler computes `deriveEffectiveRoles(context.roles).includes("owner")` — the exact function the server already uses, reused, not reimplemented (F237/G08's own precedent again) — and rejects with a new `OptimisticRejection("not-authorized")` (an open string; every prior stage has added a new value the same way, no type change) when the caller is neither that Owner check nor the target employee's manager. `conflictCheck.evaluate`'s signature gains explicit `callerUserId`/`callerRoles` fields on its existing `input` object — `bench-forecast.ts`'s own `getBenchForecast` already establishes the "named fields on `input`" shape this extends, not a new argument convention. Neither `OptimisticCache` nor `cache-data.ts`'s `localNodes`/`localEdges` gains any node-enumeration capability.

Both checks remain purely advisory locally, exactly as F259 already concluded for the write itself: the server re-verifies the manager/Owner gate authoritatively inside `overrideAndProceed` (Do item 6) regardless of what the local check decided, and a stale or wrong local read — a just-reassigned manager, a just-revoked Owner role not yet synced — is rejected there and rolled back via the same undo path already established for a capacity rejection.

This resolves F260 without reopening F255–F259.

**Status: closed.** Stage 16 is unblocked; `codex/stage-16-capacity-conflict-resolution` resumes on the corrected brief — no branch restart.

**Revised by F261, 24 September 2026.** F261 found that neither production `createGraphClient` caller — `apps/roster-web/src/components/shell-bootstrap.tsx`'s shell session or `apps/roster-web/src/app/sync-harness/sync-harness-client.tsx`'s query params — has a `roles` value to supply, and Stage 16 forbids the `apps/` edit that would be needed to add one. The `InitPayload`/`MutatorContext.roles` delivery mechanism ruled above is superseded: both the Manager and Owner checks are now derived entirely from the already-replicated `WorkspaceMembership` node and its `membership_of` edge, using operations `OptimisticCache` already exposes — no `InitPayload` change, no `MutatorContext` field, no `apps/` change, no network call. See F261's own closing ruling for the mechanism actually built.

### F261 — F260 defines `InitPayload.roles` but no production caller can supply it

Raised 24 September 2026 while tracing F260's ruled role plumbing from `MutatorContext` back to the real graph-client bootstrap, before product code was written.

**The gap.** F260 rules that `MutatorContext.roles` is threaded through `InitPayload` the same way `userId` is. `GraphClientOptions` extends `InitPayload`, so making that field real requires every `createGraphClient` caller to supply it. Neither production caller has such a value:

1. `apps/roster-web/src/components/shell-bootstrap.tsx` reads `data.user.id` and `data.session.activeOrganizationId` from Better Auth, constructs `{ workspaceId, userId }`, and calls `createGraphClient({ workspaceId, userId, apiOrigin })`. The session value exposes no membership roles, and the shell never calls `principal.current`.
2. `apps/roster-web/src/app/sync-harness/sync-harness-client.tsx` receives only `workspaceId` and `userId` query parameters, then makes the same three-field call.
3. Stage 16 Do item 11 says no file under `apps/` may change, and the Done criteria require that absence in the diff. Even if app changes were permitted, the corrected brief never defines whether the shell should extend Better Auth's session shape, call `principal.current`, read a role from the local graph, or persist a last-known role outside the graph client.

The alternatives produce materially different offline behavior. Making `roles` optional or defaulting it to `[]` lets the code compile but rejects every valid Owner override locally. Calling the existing server-side `principal.current` route during `createGraphClient` gives a current answer online, but a cold offline boot — explicitly supported by the Stage 9 shell and required by F259's fully-offline override — cannot initialize the ruled Owner check. Reading `WorkspaceMembership` from the device cache could preserve offline behavior, but F260 explicitly selected `InitPayload.roles` as an ambient session fact and forbade adding enumeration to `OptimisticCache`; the worker also needs its init payload before the engine is constructed, so that is a different bootstrap contract rather than an implementation detail.

A ruling must name the authoritative source and lifecycle for the roles supplied to `InitPayload`, including cold offline boot and role revocation, and must reconcile any required shell/harness edit with Stage 16's no-`apps/` acceptance criterion. Until then, implementing `roles` would either break compilation, silently deny Owners, or discard the ruled offline guarantee.

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 24 September 2026.** The premise was wrong, not just the delivery: `InitPayload` never needed a `roles` field at all, because the fact it exists to carry — the caller's own workspace role — is already sitting in the device cache as a plain field on an already-Tier-0, already-replicated node, reachable the same way F260's own Manager check already reaches a `managed_by` edge.

`services/api/src/graph/membership-changes.ts`'s `projectMemberAdmission`/`projectRoleChange` write `WorkspaceMembership.record.role` as `canonicalRoles(roles)` — a comma-joined, sorted `WorkspaceRole` string, the exact format `parseWorkspaceRoles` (`packages/schema/src/auth.ts`, already exported from `@vulto/schema`) decodes back to `WorkspaceRole[]`. `WorkspaceMembership` is Tier 0, Standard protection (F260), so this field is already on every device that has ever synced. The same node carries a `membership_of` edge to the caller's own `User` node (`from_node_id: membershipId, to_node_id: userId`) — the identical shape `managed_by` already has for Manager, just pointed the other way (`edgesTo`, not `edgesFrom`).

Ruled: `InitPayload`/`GraphClientOptions`/`MutatorContext` gain nothing. `overrideAndProceed`'s optimistic handler resolves Owner status with operations `OptimisticCache` already exposes — `context.cache.edgesTo(context.userId, "membership_of")` for the caller's own live membership edge, `context.cache.getNode(edge.fromNodeId)` for the `WorkspaceMembership` node, `parseWorkspaceRoles(node.record.role as string)` then `deriveEffectiveRoles(...).includes("owner")` — no enumeration, no network call, no `apps/` file, and both production `createGraphClient` callers (the shell, the sync harness) are untouched, closing F261's own named concern directly. `conflictCheck.evaluate`'s local query does the identical lookup over `localNodes`/`localEdges` once `WorkspaceMembership` and `membership_of` are added to its already-read node/edge type lists (item 4) — its `input` needs only `callerUserId`, not a separately supplied `callerRoles`, since the roles are derived from the same local cache the rest of the function already reads.

This supersedes F260's `InitPayload.roles`/`MutatorContext.roles` design (F260's own detail section is annotated to point here) without reopening F255–F259: the Owner and Manager checks stay purely advisory locally, and the server re-verifies both gates authoritatively inside `overrideAndProceed` exactly as already ruled.

**Status: closed.** Stage 16 is unblocked; `codex/stage-16-capacity-conflict-resolution` resumes on the corrected brief — no branch restart.
### F262 — F255 authorizes the Assignment node but not the two edges a Manager override must create

Raised 24 September 2026 while running Stage 16's first real `overrideAndProceed` PostgreSQL integration test through the permission interceptor, after product implementation had begun.

**The gap.** Stage 16 Do item 6 requires a target employee's Manager to invoke `conflictResolution.overrideAndProceed`, and requires that mutation to reuse `assignment.create`'s complete write path. F255 corrected `MATRIX_OVERRIDES["Assignment"].manager` from Read to unscoped Full so the new Assignment node itself can pass the write interceptor. Reusing the complete write path also creates two mandatory edges, however, and the interceptor evaluates them independently before any validation or apply step:

1. `assignment_of` joins the new Assignment to the target Employee and declares `Employee:operational` as its governing partition.
2. `assigned_to` joins the new Assignment to its Project.
3. `edgeRoleDecision` in `services/api/src/permission/interceptor.ts` requires unscoped Full on both endpoint node types. Its own comment states that no row is named during this gate, so a row-qualified grant never authorizes an edge write.
4. Manager has `Full (direct reports)` on `Employee:operational`, which is deliberately row-qualified, and only unscoped Read on Project. The Manager therefore cannot authorize either required relationship even though F255 now authorizes the Assignment endpoint.

The integration test establishes the failure through the real pipeline. A Team Member is linked to a Manager Employee, the target Employee is connected to that manager through an active `managed_by` edge, and the manager submits a valid override against a real Project. The server-side application gate accepts the caller as the target's manager, but `applyMutation` returns `{ status: "rejected", reason: "role" }` during the plan's edge checks. No Assignment is written because authorization correctly precedes validation and apply.

There is no mechanical implementation choice left by the corrected brief. Widening Manager to unscoped Full on Employee or Project would change unrelated permissions far beyond this feature. Treating a direct-report scope as sufficient for every Employee-bearing edge would require the edge interceptor to gain row context and define which endpoint establishes that scope. Omitting either edge would violate `VRS-F005`'s Assignment graph shape and Do item 6's reuse requirement. Bypassing the interceptor for mutation-derived edges would contradict `VPS-A004`'s single enforcement boundary. Restricting the override to Owner would contradict `VRS-F008`'s explicit Manager flow.

A ruling must define how a Manager-authorized Assignment creation may create its required `assignment_of` and `assigned_to` edges while preserving the interceptor's endpoint rules and the existing Employee and Project permission cells. Until then, the product implementation cannot make the promised Manager override pass without inventing a permission exception.

**Closed by founder-delegated decision, 24 September 2026 — put directly to the founder rather than traced to a single existing precedent.** Unlike F255–F261, no prior ruling decides this: `edgeRoleDecision`'s row-blind, both-endpoints-unscoped-Full rule is itself deliberate, founder-reviewed architecture (FDN-92's design memo: "the natural analogue... Full write permission required on both endpoint node types"), spanning roughly ninety registered edge types, none of which — before this feature — was ever written by a role holding anything less than unscoped Full on both its endpoints. Four alternatives were surveyed and rejected before this one: widening `Employee:operational`'s or `Project`'s Manager cell to unscoped Full changes access far beyond this feature's need, and for Employee directly contradicts `VPS-A004`'s own Role Definitions ("Full access to direct reports' operational data"); teaching `edgeRoleDecision` row context while leaving Manager's Project grant at Read would still refuse `assigned_to` outright, since Read never satisfies a Full requirement regardless of row; reusing F206's system/support principal mechanism does not fit — `SYSTEM_PRINCIPAL_OPERATIONS` is a narrow, per-job whitelist for background maintenance work (audience recompute, retention sweep, erasure, key rotation), not a general elevation path, and a support principal would misattribute a Manager's own in-product action in `AuditEntry.actor_kind`; and bypassing the interceptor for these two edges specifically would contradict `VPS-A004`'s A004-T17 three-gate sequence and the "single enforcement boundary" F138 and F206 both already treated as inviolable.

**Ruled — two additive mechanisms, one per edge, neither widening an existing grant:**

1. **`assignment_of`'s Employee endpoint: row context, not a wider grant.** `WriteTarget`'s edge variant (`services/api/src/permission/interceptor.ts`) gains optional `readonly fromNodeId?: string | null` and `readonly toNodeId?: string | null`. `edgeRoleDecision` builds a `RowScopeContext` per endpoint when an id is supplied — the identical `{ tx, principal, nodeType, nodeId, context }` shape `decideRead` already builds for reads — and passes it into `bestCell`, instead of calling `bestCell` with no row at all as it does today. Every existing edge-write call site (`managed_by`, `pattern_for`, all ~88 others) supplies no id, so its behavior is byte-for-byte unchanged. `services/api/src/mutations/assignment.ts`'s `buildAssignmentCreatePlan` — the single plan builder both `assignment.create` and `overrideAndProceed` already share (Do item 6) — already resolves `employee`/`project` as real `StoredNode`s before building `checks`; its `edgeTarget` helper gains a `toNodeId` argument, supplied as `employee.nodeId` for `assignment_of` and `project.nodeId` for `assigned_to`. With real row context, Manager's already-documented `Employee:operational` cell — `cell("full", "direct-reports", ...)`, unchanged, not widened — now correctly resolves `rowScopeSatisfied("direct-reports", row)` against the actual target employee via the same `managerOf` closure the interceptor already uses for reads: true for that employee's real manager, false otherwise. This is the interceptor's own existing machinery working for the first time in a write context that needed it, not a new privilege.
2. **`assigned_to`'s Project endpoint: read-sufficient by declaration, not a wider grant.** `Project`'s `manager` cell stays `READ_ANY()` — untouched; a Manager still cannot write a Project's own fields. `packages/schema/src/registry/edges.ts`'s `EdgeGroupShape` gains an optional `readSufficientEndpoints?: Readonly<Partial<Record<NodeType, true>>>`, declared and reviewed per edge type exactly as `governingPartitions` already is (F136/F208/F235's own precedent for this registry's opt-in, conservative-by-default style) — absent for every edge type but one, so the other ~89 are unaffected. `assigned_to` is the first and only declaration: `readSufficientEndpoints: { Project: true }`. `edgeRoleDecision` accepts an endpoint whose declared-read-sufficient cell resolves to `"read"` as well as `"full"`. This mirrors `VPS-A004`'s own already-accepted Graph traversal rule for reads — "Read on Node A, on Node B... and on the edge type" — applied here to exactly one write, not a new standard for edge writes generally.

Neither mechanism touches `Assignment`'s own endpoint (F255's `FULL_ANY()`, unchanged) or `assignment_of`'s declared governing partition (F208's `Employee: "operational"`, unchanged) — `rowScopeSatisfied("any", ...)` is unconditionally true regardless of row context, so the FROM side of both edges (Assignment, in both cases) needed neither change. This resolves F262 without reopening F255–F261.

**Status: closed.** Stage 16 is unblocked; `codex/stage-16-capacity-conflict-resolution` resumes on the corrected brief — no branch restart.

### F263 — `sweepOvercommitted` returns every overcommitted employee workspace-wide with no read-authorization filter

Raised 24 September 2026 during the post-completion review of Stage 16's finished implementation, verifying Codex's "Stage 16 is complete... No F263 was needed" report directly against the merged code rather than the report itself.

**The gap.** `services/api/src/permission/conflict-resolution-queries.ts`'s `sweepOvercommitted(tx, principal)` reads every Active `Employee` node and every Active `Assignment` node in the workspace directly via `getNodes`, computes each employee's combined capacity total through the already-reviewed `capacityTotalsFor`, and returns an `OvercommittedEmployee` entry — `employeeId`, `combinedTotal`, `overlapWindow`, `hasRecordedOverride` — for every employee over 100%, unconditionally. Neither this function nor its router wiring (`conflictResolution.sweepOvercommitted` in `services/api/src/router.ts`) narrows that list to what the calling principal may actually see: the router checks only that the requested `workspace_id` matches the caller's own workspace, and the query itself performs no per-row authorization at all.

This is the one workspace-wide node-listing query in the codebase that skips the pattern every comparable query already follows. `services/api/src/permission/employee-queries.ts`'s `listEmployees` — reading the identical `Employee` node type — pipes its rows through `filterReadable(tx, principal, rows, context)` before returning them, specifically, in that file's own words, "so a person who may not read a row does not learn it exists." `services/api/src/permission/ghost-resource-queries.ts`'s `listGhostResources` does the same for `GhostResource`. `sweepOvercommitted` is the only one of the three that omits the call.

`Employee:operational` — the partition this data is drawn from — is not an Owner/HR-admin-only cell: `packages/schema/src/policy/policy-table.ts` grants Manager `Full (direct reports)` and Team Member `Read (own + team)`, both explicitly row-scoped. `VRS-F008`'s own Interface Specification frames the sweep's output as "an admin list" reached from `VPS-F005`'s Workspace Configuration Console, and gates the *override* action explicitly to a Manager or an Owner — but the read query itself carries no such statement in the spec as briefed, and the shipped code grants it to any authenticated workspace member regardless of role or relationship: a Team Member with no reporting relationship at all to the overcommitted employee can call `conflictResolution.sweepOvercommitted` and receive every overcommitted employee in the workspace, including people they hold no `Employee:operational` read grant on whatsoever.

The integration suite does not catch this. `services/api/src/permission/capacity-conflict.integration.test.ts`'s `world()` fixture already builds an `outsider: ["team-member"]` principal specifically for this file — used elsewhere in the same file to prove an unrelated manager is refused at `assignment_of` (F262) — but every `sweepOvercommitted` call in the file, including the one proving G05's Ghost-parity and the read-only guarantee, runs only as `w.principals.owner!`. No test exercises the query as a Manager or a Team Member, so no test could have caught the missing filter.

**Closed — traced directly to the pattern already established by `listEmployees` and `listGhostResources`, no founder consultation needed.** Unlike F262, this is not an architectural fork: the exact mechanism this data needs already exists, is already applied to the same node type by a sibling query in the same permission layer, and needs no new grant, no registry change, and no widening of any policy cell.

**Ruled:** `sweepOvercommitted` gains a `context: InterceptorContext = {}` parameter matching `listEmployees`/`listGhostResources`'s own signature, and filters its `employees` array through `filterReadable(tx, principal, employees, context)` before iterating — the identical call, on the identical node type, `listEmployees` already makes. Every entry in the returned list is now exactly the set of employees the calling principal already holds `Employee:operational` read access to under the existing policy table: unscoped for Owner, HR Admin and Finance Admin; direct-reports-scoped for Manager; own-plus-team for Team Member. No cell in the policy table resolves to `"restricted"` for this partition, so `filterReadable` only ever drops or keeps a row here — it never needs to redact one, and the function's existing per-row shape is unaffected. `capacityTotalsFor`'s own per-employee assignment aggregation is unaffected: it already scopes strictly to the one employee's own assignments and was never the source of the leak. Extend `capacity-conflict.integration.test.ts` with a case calling `sweepOvercommitted` as the fixture's existing `outsider` (team-member, no relationship to the overcommitted employees) and asserting the result omits every employee the outsider cannot read, alongside one asserting a genuine Manager sees only their own direct reports among the overcommitted set.

**Status: closed.** Stage 16 is not reopened on a design question — this is a one-file correction to `sweepOvercommitted` plus new test coverage, on the existing branch, before merge.
